\displaystyle\mu\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(c,V)}

\displaystyle=4(r^{2}+a^{2})\widehat{e_{3}}\partial_{\underline{u}}+\mu\mathcal{U}^{2}+\frac{\mu}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\sin\theta\partial_{\theta})-4ias\mu\cos\theta T+2is\mu\frac{a^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\mathcal{U}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\quad+2r\mu(\widehat{e_{3}}+\partial_{\underline{u}})+4cs[(r-M)\partial_{\underline{u}}-r\mu T]+\frac{2ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi+s\mu-s^{2}\mu\frac{a^{4}\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+V,

\displaystyle=4(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{e_{3}}+\mu\mathcal{U}^{2}+\frac{\mu}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\sin\theta\partial_{\theta})-4ias\mu\cos\theta T+2is\mu\frac{a^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\mathcal{U}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\quad+2r\mu(\widehat{e_{3}}+\partial_{\underline{u}})+4cs[(r-M)\partial_{\underline{u}}-r\mu T]-\frac{2ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi+s\mu-s^{2}\mu\frac{a^{4}\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+V.

Note that we used here

4(r^{2}+a^{2})[\widehat{e_{3}},\partial_{\underline{u}}]=-\frac{4ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi.

Next, similarly as in
[
24
]
for the scalar wave equation, we multiply the modified inhomogeneous Teukolsky equation (
4.7
) by
\mu
and by the complex conjugate of
X(\widehat{\psi}):=f(r)\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}+g(r)\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi},
where

f(r):=|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha},\quad g(r)=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p},

with
p=p(a,M)\gg 1
and
\alpha>1
that will be chosen later, and
r_{\mathfrak{b}}=r_{\mathfrak{b}}(a,M)
that will be chosen sufficiently close to
r_{-}
. We take the real part, and integrate on
S(u,{\underline{u}})
with respect to
\mathrm{d}\nu
to get

\displaystyle\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}\left(f(r)\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mu\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(c,V)}\widehat{\psi}\right)+\mathfrak{R}\left(g(r)\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mu\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(c,V)}\widehat{\psi}\right)\mathrm{d}\nu=\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mu\mathfrak{R}\left(\overline{X(\widehat{\psi})}O({\underline{u}}^{-\beta})\right)\mathrm{d}\nu.

(4.10)

By Lemma
A.1
, this can be rewritten

\displaystyle\partial_{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\right)+\partial_{u}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\right)+\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu=\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mu\mathfrak{R}\left(\overline{X(\widehat{\psi})}O({\underline{u}}^{-\beta})\right)\mathrm{d}\nu,

(4.11)

where
\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]
,
\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]
and
\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]
are defined in Lemma
A.1
. As in
[
24
]
, integrating (
4.11
) on
\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\}\cap\mathbf{II}
with respect to
\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}{\underline{u}}
gives

\displaystyle\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{w}}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle+\iint_{\Gamma\cap\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}}\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\underline{u}+\iiint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{w}}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u+\iint_{\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}}\mathcal{T}_{r}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

\displaystyle\quad+\iiint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mu\mathfrak{R}\left(\overline{X(\widehat{\psi})}O({\underline{u}}^{-\beta})\right)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u},

(4.12)

where
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{w}}[\widehat{\psi}]=\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\mu\right)\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]-\frac{1}{2}\mu\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]
,
\mathcal{T}_{r}[\widehat{\psi}]=-\frac{1}{2}\mu\left(\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]+\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]\right)
, and
\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}[\widehat{\psi}]=\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]+(1-\gamma{\underline{u}}^{\gamma-1})\mathbf{F}_{{{\underline{u}}}}[\widehat{\psi}]
.
Now we estimate the different quantities in (
4.12
) taking into account the choice of
f,g
.

Step 1: control of the bulk terms.
We begin by proving that
\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]
controls
\mathbf{e}_{\alpha+1}[\widehat{\psi}]
in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
using a blueshift energy estimate. This estimate is present for the spin
-2
rescaled Teukolsky operator
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{(c,V)}
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
. It is also present for the spin
+2
Teukolsky operator near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
, where it is stronger in the sense that in that case, the bulk term controls the non-degenerate energy
\mathbf{e}[\psi]
, see
[
15
]
. By Lemma
A.2
, we get that for
\alpha>1
,
s=-2
,
c>0
,
V\in\mathbb{R}
, for
p(a,M)>0
large enough, and
r_{\mathfrak{b}}(p,a,M)
close enough to
r_{-}
, we have in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\gtrsim(-\mu)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha+1}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu.

(4.13)

Next we write, for
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\iiint

{}_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mu\mathfrak{R}(\overline{X(\widehat{\psi})}O({\underline{u}}^{-\beta}))\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\Bigg|\lesssim

(4.14)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\iiint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}|X(\widehat{\psi})|^{2}(-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}+\varepsilon^{-1}\iiint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}{\underline{u}}^{-2\beta}(-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}.

Changing variables
15
15

15

On
\underline{w}=cst
, we have
\mu\mathrm{d}u=(2-\mu)\mathrm{d}r
.
from
u
to
r
, and using compactness in
r
, we get

\iiint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}{\underline{u}}^{-2\beta}(-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\lesssim\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}{\underline{u}}^{-2\beta}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\lesssim\int_{\underline{w}_{1}}^{\underline{w}_{2}}\underline{w}^{-2\beta}\mathrm{d}{\underline{u}}.

As
\mu^{2}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}\lesssim(-\mu)|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha-1}
in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
, we have

|X(\widehat{\psi})|^{2}\lesssim\mu^{2}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}|e_{3}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\lesssim\mathbf{e}_{\alpha+1}[\widehat{\psi}].

Combining this fact with (
4.13
) and (
4.12
), taking
\varepsilon>0
small enough such that the first term on the RHS of (

) is absorbed in the LHS of (
4.12

, we get

\displaystyle\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{w}}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u+\iint_{\Gamma\cap\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}}\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\underline{u}+\iiint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha+1}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{w}}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u+\iint_{\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}}\mathcal{T}_{r}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\underline{u}+\int_{\underline{w}_{1}}^{\underline{w}_{2}}\underline{w}^{-2\beta}\mathrm{d}{\underline{u}}.

(4.15)

As in
[
24
, (4.19)]
, using the definition of region
\mathbf{II}
we get

\mathbf{e}_{\alpha+1}[\widehat{\psi}]\gtrsim|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}]\sim(r^{*})^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}]\gtrsim\underline{w}^{-\gamma}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}]

in
\mathbf{II}
which implies, re-injecting in (
4.15
),

\displaystyle\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{w}}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u+\iint_{\Gamma\cap\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}}\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\underline{u}+\iiint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\underline{w}^{-\gamma}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{w}}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u+\iint_{\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}}\mathcal{T}_{r}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\underline{u}+\int_{\underline{w}_{1}}^{\underline{w}_{2}}\underline{w}^{-2\beta}\mathrm{d}{\underline{u}}.

(4.16)

Step 2: control of the boundary terms.

The integrated term on
\underline{w}=cst
is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\underline{w}}[\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle=\left(1-\mu/2\right)\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]-\dfrac{1}{2}\mu\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle=2(r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\mu g(r)(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})+a\sin\theta\mu\mathfrak{R}(X(\widehat{\psi})\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{\mu}{2}\Bigg[2(r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+2(r^{2}+a^{2})g(r)|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\mu(f(r)+g(r))(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\sim(-\mu)\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}],

where we absorbed the term
a\sin\theta\mu\mathfrak{R}(X(\widehat{\psi})\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})
as in
[
24
]
using

\displaystyle|a\sin\theta\mu f(r)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})|

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}f(r)|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\mu^{2}f(r)|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2},

\displaystyle|a\sin\theta\mu g(r)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})|

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{3}{4}\mu a^{2}g(r)|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}-\frac{1}{3}\mu g(r)|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}.

Then, we have

\mathcal{T}_{r}[\widehat{\psi}]=-\dfrac{1}{2}\mu\left(\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]+\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]\right)\sim\mathbf{e}[\widehat{\psi}],\text{ on }\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}.

Combining this control of the boundary terms with (
4.16
) concludes the proof of Proposition
4.5
.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Assume that
\widehat{\psi}
is a spin
-2
scalar satisfying (
4.6
) and (
4.7
). Then for
\alpha>1
and
\gamma>0
small enough we have, for
0\leq j\leq N_{j}^{\prime}
,
0\leq 2k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N_{k}^{\prime}
:

\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}={\underline{w}}_{1}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\underline{w}_{1}^{-2\beta-2j+\gamma}.

Proof.

Once again, it suffices to prove the case
j=k=0
. Proposition
4.5
and the energy assumption (
4.6
) on
\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
gives

\displaystyle\iint

{}_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u+\iiint_{\left\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\underline{w}^{-\gamma}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

(4.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u+\underline{w}_{1}^{-2\beta}+\int_{\underline{w}_{1}}^{\underline{w}_{2}}\underline{w}^{-2\beta}\mathrm{d}\underline{u},

where we used the fact that the term on
\Gamma
can be chosen non negative as

\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}[\widehat{\psi}]=\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]+(1-\gamma{\underline{u}}^{\gamma-1})\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{{{\underline{u}}}}[\widehat{\psi}]\geq f(r)|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+g(r)|e_{4}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}-\mu(f(r)+g(r))(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})\geq 0

for
\gamma>0
small enough. Using the fact that for any
\underline{w}_{1}\geq 1
, the energy

\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\lesssim 1

is finite
16
16

16

In the notation of Lemma
2.15
, this means that
f(1)
is finite. This holds because
\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{II}
is a compact region inside a globally hyperbolic spacetime and by standard existence results for linear wave equations with smooth initial data.
, we conclude the proof using Lemma
2.15
with
p=2\beta
.
∎

The following result will be used to deduce decay in
L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))
from energy decay.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\psi
be a spin
\pm 2
scalar. We define the scalar function

f(\underline{w},r):=\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(S(u(r,\underline{w}),{\underline{u}}(r,\underline{w})))}.

Then for
\underline{w}\geq 1
and
r_{2}\leq r_{1}
such that
(\underline{w},r_{1}),(\underline{w},r_{2})\in\mathbf{II}
,

f(\underline{w},r_{2})\lesssim f(\underline{w},r_{1})+\underline{w}^{\frac{1}{2}\gamma(\alpha+1)}\left(\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\psi]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}.

Proof.

In coordinates
({\underline{u}},r,\theta,\phi_{+})
we have
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}=-2e_{3}
,
\partial_{{\underline{u}}}=T
.
Thus for a fixed
\underline{w}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial r}(f(\underline{w},r))=\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial r}(\underline{w}+r-r_{+})\partial_{{\underline{u}}_{\mathrm{in}}}+\frac{\partial r}{\partial r}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\right]f({\underline{u}},r)=(\partial_{{\underline{u}}_{\mathrm{in}}}+\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}})f({\underline{u}},r).

(4.18)

Moreover, by a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle|(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}+\partial_{{\underline{u}}})(f^{2})|

\displaystyle=\left|(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}+\partial_{{\underline{u}}})\left(\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\psi|^{2}({\underline{u}},r,\theta,\phi_{+})\sin\theta\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\phi_{+}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|2\mathfrak{R}\left(\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(\overline{\psi}(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}+\partial_{{\underline{u}}})\psi)({\underline{u}},r,\theta,\phi_{+})\sin\theta\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\phi_{+}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2f\|(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}+\partial_{{\underline{u}}})\psi\|_{L^{2}(S(\underline{w},r))}=2f\|(T-2e_{3})\psi\|_{L^{2}(S(\underline{w},r))},

which yields

\displaystyle|(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}+\partial_{{\underline{u}}})f|=\frac{1}{2f}|(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}+\partial_{{\underline{u}}})(f^{2})|\lesssim\|(T-2e_{3})\psi\|_{L^{2}(S(\underline{w},r))}.

(4.19)

Integrating (
4.18
), together with the bound (
4.19
) gives

\displaystyle f(\underline{w},r_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim f(\underline{w},r_{1})+\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\left(\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}|T\psi-2e_{3}\psi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim f(\underline{w},r_{1})+\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\left(\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}|T\psi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\left(\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}|e_{3}\psi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(4.20)

Next, we use the expression (
2.16
) which gives
T=O(\mu)e_{3}+O(1)e_{4}+O(1)\mathcal{U}+O(1)
. Using
\mu^{2}\lesssim-\mu|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha}
and the Poincaré inequality (
2.20
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}|T\psi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\lesssim\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\psi]\mathrm{d}\nu.

(4.21)

Now we treat the last term on the RHS of (
4.20
) which is more difficult to bound. Using a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we get

\displaystyle\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\Bigg(\int_{S(\underline{w}_{1},r)}

\displaystyle|e_{3}\psi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}(-\mu)^{-1}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}(-\mu)|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha}|e_{3}\psi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}(-\mu)^{-1}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\psi]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}.

(4.22)

All is left to do is to estimate the first integral on the RHS of (
4.22
). We have in
\mathbf{II}
,

|{\log(-\mu)}|\sim r^{*}\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{\gamma}\lesssim\underline{w}^{\gamma}.

We also have

|u_{r_{2}}(\underline{w})-u_{r_{1}}(\underline{w})|=|2r^{*}-r-2r_{\mathfrak{b}}^{*}+r_{\mathfrak{b}}|\lesssim\underline{w}^{\gamma}

in
\mathbf{II}
. This gives

\int_{r}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(-\mu)^{-1}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\int_{u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(\underline{w})}^{u_{r}(\underline{w})}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\underline{w}^{\gamma(\alpha+1)}

and concludes the proof of Lemma
4.7
.
∎

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\widehat{\psi}
be a spin
-2
scalar satisfying (
4.5
), (
4.6
) and (
4.7
). Then for
\gamma>0
small enough we have in
\mathbf{II}
, for
0\leq j\leq N_{j}^{\prime}-2
and
0\leq 2k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N_{k}^{\prime}-2
:

|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\widehat{\psi}|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-\beta-j+C_{\alpha}\gamma},

where
C_{\alpha}=\alpha/2+1
.

Proof.

It is enough to treat the case
j=k_{1}=k_{2}=0
. The loss of two derivatives will be due to the use of the Sobolev embedding (
2.19
). Using Lemma
4.7
between
r(u,{\underline{u}})
and
r_{\mathfrak{b}}
, and Proposition
4.6
gives

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}\lesssim\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(S(u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(\underline{w}),{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(\underline{w}))}+\underline{w}^{\frac{1}{2}\gamma(\alpha+1)}\left(\iint_{\left\{\underline{w}={\underline{w}(u,{\underline{u}})}\right\}\cap\mathbf{II}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{s}^{k}\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\right)^{1/2}.

Thus denoting
C_{\alpha}=\alpha/2+1
we have shown, using also (
4.5
) to control the
L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))
norm on
\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}\lesssim\underline{w}^{-\beta+C_{\alpha}\gamma}.

(4.23)

Together with the Sobolev embedding (
2.19
) and the
j,k_{1}\neq 0
version of (
4.23
), we infer

|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\lesssim\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}\widehat{\psi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+\|T^{\leq 2}\widehat{\psi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}\lesssim\underline{w}_{1}^{-2\beta+2C_{\alpha}\gamma},

as stated. This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.8
.
∎

4.2
Upper bounds for
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
in intermediate region
\mathbf{II}

In this section, we prove a non-sharp upper bound for
T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
in region
\mathbf{II}
. To this end, we use the estimates from Section
4.1
and the bounds (
3.2
), (
3.3
) in
\mathbf{I}
that were deduced from assumption (
3.1
) on the event horizon. The following result is a simple computation:

Proposition 4.9
.

We have in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
, for
j,k_{1},k_{2}\geq 0
,

\left|\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)\right|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-7-\delta-j}.

Proof.

We have, using the definition of the rescaled operator
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\Delta^{2}\mathbf{T}_{-2}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\Delta^{2}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{t}]}\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\Delta{\underline{u}}^{-8-j}\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta},

where we used the computation in (
3.4
) in the third line.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

Note that to get the bound in Proposition
4.9
, we used the estimate
|\Delta|\lesssim 1
in
\mathbf{II}
, which is too imprecise to derive a sharp lower bound for
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
. This section is in fact only devoted to proving a somewhat precise upper bound for the
T
derivatives of
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
(see Proposition
4.11
below), and the precise lower bound will be obtained in Section
5
.

Proposition 4.11
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). For
\gamma>0
small enough, we have in
\mathbf{II}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}],

where for
0\leq j\leq N_{j}-4
and
0\leq 2k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N_{k}-5
,

|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta/2}.

Remark 4.12
.

The result of Proposition
4.11
only gives a sharp lower bound for
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
in any subregion of
\mathbf{II}
where
r
is bounded away from
r_{-}
. This lower bound degenerates at
\Gamma
where
\Delta^{2}/{\underline{u}}^{7}
is much smaller than
{\underline{u}}^{-7-\delta/2}
(
\Delta
decays exponentially in
{\underline{u}}
on
\Gamma
).

Proof of Proposition
4.11
.

We recall

\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=\widehat{\psi}_{-2}-\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}.

We have, combining the Teukolsky equation
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=0
, (
2.24
) and Proposition
4.9
:

\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=O({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

Thus using the pointwise bound (
3.2
), the energy bound (
3.3
) on
\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
, (
4.3
) and Proposition
4.8
with
c=1>0
,
V=0
,
\beta=7+\delta
, gives the result without the
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}
derivative, i.e.

\displaystyle|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta+C_{\alpha}\gamma}\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta/2}

(4.24)

where
\alpha=3/2
, for
\gamma>0
small enough. Now we prove

\displaystyle|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta/2}.

(4.25)

Using Proposition
2.19
for the expression of
[\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2},\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}]
, and Proposition
4.9

we find that
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
satisfies, in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
:

\displaystyle\Big(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}+4[(r-M)\mu^{-1}e_{4}-rT]-2\Big)

\displaystyle T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=

\displaystyle-6T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}T\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]+O({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

(4.26)

Thus using (
4.24
), as well as the expression (
4.2
) of the modified Teukolsky operator, we get

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{(1/2,-2)}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=O({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

(4.27)

We now prove that
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
satisfies (
4.5
) and (
4.6
). This comes from the identity

\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}=-2e_{3}+O(1)T+O(1)\Phi,\quad\text{ on }\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\},

as well as the bounds (
3.2
), (
3.3
). We conclude the proof using Proposition
4.8
for
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
with
c=1/2>0
,
V=-2
,
\beta=7+\delta
and
\gamma>0
small enough.
∎

4.3
Energy method for
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}
in region
\mathbf{III}

Sections
4.3
and
4.4
are dedicated to propagating the bounds of Proposition
4.11
from region
\mathbf{II}
to region
\mathbf{III}
. This will be achieved using, in region
\mathbf{III}
, a version of the energy method which is more naive than the one used in
\mathbf{II}
. We now implement this energy method. In this section,
\alpha>1
is fixed.

In this section we consider a spin
-2
scalar
\widehat{\psi}
such that there is
B\in\mathbb{R}
,
c>0
,
V\in\mathbb{R}
such that:

\displaystyle\bullet\>

\displaystyle\iint_{\Gamma\cap\{\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{2}\}}\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}{\underline{u}}\lesssim 1\text{ for any }1\leq\underline{w}_{1}\leq\underline{w}_{2},

(4.28)

\displaystyle\bullet\>

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{(c,V)}\widehat{\psi}=O({\underline{u}}^{B})\text{ in }\mathbf{III},

(4.29)

recalling the expression of
\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
from Proposition
4.5
.

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
\widehat{\psi}
be a spin
-2
scalar satisfying (
4.28
) and (
4.29
). Then for
\underline{w}_{1}\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\iint_{\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r\lesssim 1+\int_{\underline{w}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\!(w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma})}^{\underline{w}_{1}}\underline{w}^{2B}\mathrm{d}\underline{w};

Proof.

We use the same energy method as in region
\mathbf{II}
, but this time we integrate (
4.11
) with
\beta=-B
on
\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma},\>\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}
. Using Lemma
A.2
and the bound equivalent to (
4.14
) in
\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma},\>\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}
for
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we get similarly as in (
4.15
):

\displaystyle\iint_{\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma},\>\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}{\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}}[\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u+\iint_{\{\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{1},\>w=w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathcal{T}_{w}[\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}{\underline{u}}

\displaystyle+\iiint_{\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma},\>\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha+1}[\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}{\underline{u}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\iint_{\Gamma\cap\{\underline{w}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\!(w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma})\leq\underline{w}\leq\underline{w}_{1}\}}\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}{\underline{u}}+\int_{\underline{w}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\!(w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma})}^{\underline{w}_{1}}\underline{w}^{2B}\mathrm{d}\underline{w},

(4.30)

where, as in
[
24
]
, the boundary term on
\{w=w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
is

\mathcal{T}_{w}[\widehat{\psi}]:=(1-\mu/2)\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]-(\mu/2)\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]\sim|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}-\mu|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha}(|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})\geq 0.

Thus from (
4.28
) we deduce, dropping two non-negative terms on the LHS of (
4.30
),

\iint_{\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}{\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}}[\widehat{\psi}](-\mu)\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}u\lesssim 1+\int_{\underline{w}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\!(w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma})}^{\underline{w}_{1}}\underline{w}^{2B}\mathrm{d}\underline{w},

and we conclude the proof changing variables from
u
to
r
in the integral on the left.
∎

4.4
Upper bound for
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
in region
\mathbf{III}

In this section, using the naive non-decaying energy estimate of Section
4.3
, we first find a polynomial upper bound for
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
in
\mathbf{III}
. Commuting the Teukolsky equation with
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}
and applying the energy method again, this will give a polynomial upper bound for
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
in
\mathbf{III}
, which, integrated from
\Gamma
, will propagate the
O(|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2})
decay of
T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
from
\Gamma
to
\mathbf{III}
, using the exponential decay of
\Delta
in
{\underline{u}}
in
\mathbf{III}
. Finally, we integrate a 1+1 wave equation extracted from the Teukolsky equation to prove the
O(|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2})
decay of
T^{j}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
in
\mathbf{III}
.

Proposition 4.14
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). We have in
\mathbf{III}
, for
j,k_{1},k_{2}\geq 0
, and
\alpha>1
,

\displaystyle\iint_{\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r\lesssim 1.

Proof.

Using Proposition
4.9
and Proposition
4.5
with
\widehat{\psi}=\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
and
\beta=7+\delta
, as well as Proposition
4.6
, we get that
T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
satisfies (
4.28
) and (
4.29
) with
B=-7-\delta
. Thus, using Proposition
4.13
, we get

\displaystyle\iint_{\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r\lesssim 1+\int_{\underline{w}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\!(w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma})}^{\underline{w}_{1}}\underline{w}^{-2(7+\delta)}\mathrm{d}\underline{w}\lesssim 1,

which concludes the proof.
∎

The following result will be used to deduce polynomial upper bounds from the energy estimate of Proposition
4.14
.

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
\psi
be a spin
\pm 2
scalar. We define the scalar function

f(\underline{w},r):=\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(S(u(r,\underline{w}),{\underline{u}}(r,\underline{w})))}.

Then for
\underline{w}\geq 1
and
r_{2}\leq r_{1}
such that
(\underline{w},r_{1}),(\underline{w},r_{2})\in\mathbf{III}
,

f(\underline{w},r_{2})\lesssim f(\underline{w},r_{1})+\underline{w}^{\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+1)}\left(\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\psi]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}.

Proof.

Recall (
4.20
) and (
4.22
) which yield

\displaystyle f(\underline{w},r_{2})\lesssim

\displaystyle\>f(\underline{w},r_{1})+\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\left(\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}|T\psi|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}(-\mu)^{-1}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{1}}\int_{S(\underline{w},r^{\prime})}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[\psi]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}.

(4.31)

By (
4.21
), it is only left to estimate the first integral on the second line of (
4.31
). We have in
\mathbf{III}
,

|{\log(-\mu)}|\sim r^{*}\sim u+{\underline{u}}\lesssim\underline{w}

for
\underline{w}\gtrsim 1
large enough. We also have

|u_{r_{2}}(\underline{w})-u_{r_{1}}(\underline{w})|=|2r^{*}-r-2r_{\mathfrak{b}}^{*}+r_{\mathfrak{b}}|\lesssim\underline{w}

in
\mathbf{III}
for
\underline{w}\gtrsim 1
large enough. This gives

\int_{r}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(-\mu)^{-1}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\int_{u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(\underline{w})}^{u_{r}(\underline{w})}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\underline{w}^{\alpha+1}

and concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.16
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). Then, in
\mathbf{III}
, for
0\leq j\leq N_{j}-4
and
0\leq 2k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N_{k}-5
, and
\alpha>1
,

|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+1)}.

Proof.

We begin by proving the bound in
L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))
norm. We use Lemma
4.15
to get in
\mathbf{III}
:

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]\|_{L^{2}(S(u_{\Gamma}(\underline{w}),{\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(\underline{w})))}+\underline{w}^{\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+1)}\left(\iint_{\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underline{w}^{\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+1)}\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+1)},

where we used Proposition
4.11
to bound the term on
\Gamma
and Proposition
4.14
to bound the energy term. Next, using the Sobolev embedding (
2.19
), we get

|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|^{2}\lesssim\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|T^{\leq j+2}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu+\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}+1}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{\alpha+1},

which concludes the proof. The loss of derivatives is the same as the one in Proposition
4.11
because, although we use the Sobolev embedding (
2.19
), we only use the decay of
\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]
on
\Gamma
in
L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))
, which saves two angular and
T
derivatives.
∎

We now turn to getting the polynomial upper bound for
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
in
\mathbf{III}
.

Proposition 4.17
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). Then, we have in
\mathbf{III}
, for
0\leq j\leq N_{j}-2
and
0\leq 2k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N_{k}-3
, and
\alpha>1
,

\displaystyle\iint_{\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\underline{w}_{1}^{\alpha+2}.

Proof.

Recall the equation (
4.26
) satisfied by
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
. Using Proposition
4.16
, it rewrites
17
17

17

We save two angular and
T
derivatives as in the energy method we only use the bound for the RHS of (
4.32
) in
L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))
norm.

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{(1/2,-2)}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=O({\underline{u}}^{\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+1)}).

(4.32)

This proves that
T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
satisfies (
4.29
) with
B=(\alpha+1)/2
,
c=1/2>0
,
V=-2
. Moreover,
T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
satisfies (
4.28
) by Propositions
4.5
and
4.6
with
\beta=7+j+\delta
. Thus, by Proposition
4.13
we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\int_{\underline{w}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\!(w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma})}^{\underline{w}_{1}}\underline{w}^{\alpha+1}\mathrm{d}\underline{w}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underline{w}_{1}^{\alpha+2}

for
\underline{w}_{1}\geq 1
large enough.
∎

Proposition 4.18
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). We have in
\mathbf{III}
, for
0\leq j\leq N_{j}-4
and
0\leq 2k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N_{k}-5
, and
\alpha>1
,

|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{3/2+\alpha}.

Proof.

We use Lemma
4.15
and Proposition
4.17
to get

\displaystyle\|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]\|_{L^{2}(S(u_{\Gamma}(\underline{w}),{\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(\underline{w})))}

\displaystyle\quad+\underline{w}^{\frac{1}{2}(\alpha+1)}\left(\iint_{\{\underline{w}=\underline{w}_{1}\}\cap\mathbf{III}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]]\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underline{w}^{3/2+\alpha}\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{3/2+\alpha},

where we used Proposition
4.11
to bound the term on
\Gamma
. As before, we conclude with the Sobolev embedding (
2.19
).
∎

We now deduce actual decay from the estimate in Proposition
4.18
and the identity
\partial_{\underline{u}}=(\mu/2)\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}
, as well as the fact that
\mu
decays exponentially in
{\underline{u}}
in
\mathbf{III}
. Indeed, recall the identity
-\Delta\sim\exp(-2|\kappa_{-}|r^{*})
in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
. The definition of region
\mathbf{III}
then implies

\displaystyle-\Delta\lesssim\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|{\underline{u}}^{\gamma})\text{ in }\mathbf{III}.

(4.33)

Proposition 4.19
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). We have in
\mathbf{III}
, for
0\leq j\leq N_{j}-4
and
0\leq 2k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N_{k}-5
,

|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}.

Proof.

Using Proposition
4.18
and
\partial_{\underline{u}}=e_{4}=(\mu/2)\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}
, defining
K=3/2+\alpha
, we get in
\mathbf{III}
:

|\partial_{\underline{u}}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim-\Delta{\underline{u}}^{K}\lesssim\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|{\underline{u}}^{\gamma}){\underline{u}}^{K}.

This yields, integrating from
\Gamma
on
u=cst
,
\phi_{-}=cst
,
\theta=cst
:

\displaystyle|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}](u,{\underline{u}},\theta,\phi_{-})

\displaystyle-T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}](u,{\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(u),\theta,\phi_{-})|

(4.34)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(u)}^{{\underline{u}}}\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{{\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(u)}^{+\infty}\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K}\mathrm{d}x.

Integrating by parts, we have for any
A\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\int_{A}^{+\infty}

\displaystyle\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{\gamma|\kappa_{-}|}\int_{A}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}[\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})]x^{K-\gamma+1}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{\gamma|\kappa_{-}|}\Bigg[-\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|A^{\gamma})A^{K-\gamma+1}-(K-\gamma+1)\int_{A}^{+\infty}\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K-\gamma}\mathrm{d}x\Bigg].

As
\gamma>0
,
\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K-\gamma}=o(\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K})
as
x\to\infty
thus

\int_{A}^{+\infty}\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K-\gamma}\mathrm{d}x=o\left(\int_{A}^{+\infty}\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K}\mathrm{d}x\right)

as
A\to+\infty
, which proves

\int_{A}^{+\infty}\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|x^{\gamma})x^{K}\mathrm{d}x\underset{A\to+\infty}{\sim}\frac{\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|A^{\gamma})A^{K-\gamma+1}}{\gamma|\kappa_{-}|}.

This implies, together with (
4.34
),

\displaystyle|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}](u,{\underline{u}},\theta,\phi_{-})

\displaystyle-T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}](u,{\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(u),\theta,\phi_{-})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-|\kappa_{-}|{\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(u)^{\gamma}){\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(u)^{K-\gamma+1}\lesssim|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2},

where we used Lemma
2.21
which gives
{\underline{u}}_{\Gamma}(u)\sim|u|
. Combining this bound with Proposition
4.11
and Lemma
2.21
, which proves that the term on
\Gamma
is bounded by
|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}
, concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.20
.

The previous proof also implies that
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
has a limit on
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
.

The bound in Proposition
4.19
is the desired
goal of this section, for
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
. More precisely, we will use Proposition
4.19
with
j\geq 1
, i.e. the fact that
T
derivatives of
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
decay at least like
|u|^{-8-\delta/2}
. The rest of this section is dedicated to showing that this bound also holds for
T
derivatives of
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
. This relies on the integration of a 1+1 wave equation extracted from the Teukolsky equation, similarly as what was done for
\widehat{\psi}_{+2}
in
[
15
]
.

Proposition 4.21
.

Choosing
\gamma>0
small enough, we have in
\mathbf{III}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}],

where for
0\leq j\leq N_{j}-5
and
0\leq 2k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N_{k}-7
,

|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}.

Proof.

The bound without the
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}
derivative has been proven in Proposition
4.19
, so we prove the bound with the
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}
. We begin by rewriting the Teukolsky equation
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=O({\underline{u}}^{-8})
as a 1+1 wave equation with a right-hand-side that we will control using Proposition
4.19
. Using Propositions
2.17
and
4.9
we get

\displaystyle\widehat{e_{3}}((r^{2}+a^{2})\Delta^{-2}e_{4}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])=\frac{1}{4}

\displaystyle(-\mu)\Delta^{-2}(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}+2aT\Phi+6rT)[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]]

\displaystyle+O(-\Delta^{-1}{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

We can rewrite this as

\displaystyle\widehat{e_{3}}((r^{2}+a^{2})

\displaystyle\Delta^{-2}e_{4}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Delta^{-1}}{4(r^{2}+a^{2})}(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}+2aT\Phi+6rT)[T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]]+O(-\Delta^{-1}{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

Thus using
\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}=2\mu^{-1}e_{4}
and Propositions
4.19
and
4.11
, as well as
{\underline{u}}\sim|u|
in
\mathbf{II}
and
{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}\lesssim|u|^{-7-j-\delta}
in
\mathbf{III}
by Lemma
2.21
, this implies in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
:

\displaystyle\widehat{e_{3}}(\Delta^{-1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])=O(-\Delta^{-1}|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}).

(4.35)

We integrate (
4.35
) on integral curves of
\widehat{e_{3}}
from
\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
to
(u,{\underline{u}},\theta,\phi_{-})
where
{\underline{u}},\theta,\phi_{+}
are constant. Using
\widehat{e_{3}}=\partial_{u}
in these coordinates, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta^{-1}(u,{\underline{u}})\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]

\displaystyle(u,{\underline{u}},\theta,\phi_{-})=

\displaystyle\Delta^{-1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}](u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}},\theta,(\phi_{-})_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u,{\underline{u}},\phi_{-}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}({\underline{u}})}^{u}O(-\Delta^{-1}(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})|u^{\prime}|^{-7-j-\delta/2})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

(4.36)

We now estimate the last term on the RHS. We have, on
{\underline{u}}=cst
,

\partial_{u}\Delta^{-1}=\widehat{e_{3}}\Delta^{-1}=(\mu/2)\partial_{r}\Delta^{-1}=-\frac{r-M}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Delta^{-1}.

This gives

\displaystyle\int_{u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\Delta^{-1}(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})|u^{\prime}|^{-7-j-\delta/2}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\sim\int_{u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\partial_{u}\Delta^{-1}(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})|u^{\prime}|^{-7-j-\delta/2}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\sim\Delta^{-1}(u,{\underline{u}})|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}-\Delta^{-1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})|u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}({\underline{u}})|^{-7-j-\delta/2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(7+j+\delta)\int_{u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\Delta^{-1}(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})|u^{\prime}|^{-8-j-\delta/2}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

and thus for
|u|
large enough we get

\int_{u_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}({\underline{u}})}^{u}O(-\Delta^{-1}(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})|u^{\prime}|^{-7-j-\delta/2})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\lesssim-\Delta^{-1}(u,{\underline{u}})|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}.

Going back to (
4.36
) and using (
3.2
) to bound the term on
\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
, this gives in
\mathbf{III}
,

\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=O(-\Delta{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta})+O(|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2})=O(|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}),

which concludes the proof.
∎

5
Precise asymptotics of
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}

In this section, we begin by finding the precise asymptotics of the
\ell=2
modes of
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
, proving Theorem
3.5
. Then, we continue this section by proving a
O(|u|^{-8-\delta}+r-r_{-})
upper bound for the
\ell\geq 3
modes of
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
, separating the analysis between the modes
\ell=3
and
\ell\geq 4
, which proves Theorems
3.6
and
3.7
. The analysis is done by using the
(\partial_{t},\partial_{r})
-decomposition (
2.28
) of the Teukolsky operator to integrate simple ODEs in
r
with source terms that can be controlled using the estimates of Section
4
.

In Sections
5.1
,
5.2
,
5.3
,
5.4
, we write as follows the ingoing spin-weighted spherical harmonics decomposition, for
\ell=2,3
:

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell}=\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}F_{m,\ell}(r,{\underline{u}})Y_{m,\ell}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}},

(5.1)

where the functions
F_{m,\ell}(r,{\underline{u}})
are smooth. Note that this implies, for
j\geq 0
,

\displaystyle(T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell}=\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,\ell}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})Y_{m,\ell}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}.

(5.2)

We also define the functions

\displaystyle G_{m,\ell}(r,u):=\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}F_{m,\ell}(r,2r^{*}-u),

(5.3)

such that we have the outgoing spin-weighted spherical harmonics decomposition

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell}=\sum_{|m|\leq 2}G_{m,\ell}(r,u)Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{-}}.

(5.4)

We also define for convenience the functions

\displaystyle{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r):=\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,\ell}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}}),\quad g_{m,\ell,u}:=G_{m,\ell}(r,u).

(5.5)

5.1
Precise asymptotics for
(\psi_{-2})_{\ell=2}
in region
\mathbf{I}

We begin the proof of Theorem
3.5
with the following result.

Proposition 5.1
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). Then the functions
F_{m,2}(r,{\underline{u}})
satisfy in
\mathbf{I}
the following equations, for
|m|\leq 2
:

\displaystyle\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r^{2}}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})+(2iam+6(r-M))\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})=P_{m}(r)\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}),

(5.6)

where, recalling the definition of the constants
b_{m,\ell}^{s}
in Proposition
2.7
,

\displaystyle P_{m}(r):=14Q_{m,2}(5r+iam+2iab_{m,2}^{-2}).

(5.7)

Remark 5.2
.

The notations
\frac{\partial}{\partial r}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial{\underline{u}}}
applied to
F_{m,2}(r,{\underline{u}})
stand respectively for the derivative with respect to the first and second variables of
F_{m,2}(r,{\underline{u}})
, and not to some Boyer-Lindquist or Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates vector fields.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Using (
3.2
) we have in
\mathbf{I}
:

\displaystyle\psi_{-2}=\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}],

(5.8)

where, in particular:

\displaystyle|T^{2}(T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-9-j-\delta},\quad|T(T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta},

\displaystyle|T\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}(T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta},\quad|T\Phi(T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}.

By the expression (
2.30
) of
\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{t}]}
this implies in
\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle|\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{t}]}T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}.

(5.9)

Moreover, using (
5.8
), the Teukolsky equation writes

\mathbf{T}_{-2}T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]=-T^{j}\mathbf{T}_{-2}\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right),

thus using (
5.9
) and the
(\partial_{t},\partial_{r})
-decomposition (
2.28
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{[\partial_{r}]}_{-2}T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]=-T^{j}\mathbf{T}_{-2}\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.10)

Now we compute using (
2.29
), (
2.30
), as well as

\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}{\underline{u}}=\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\phi_{+}=\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\theta=\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}(Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}})=0,

the source term:

\displaystyle-T^{j}\mathbf{T}_{-2}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)=

\displaystyle T^{j}\left(\frac{14}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}(5r+2ia\cos\theta+iam)Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O({\underline{u}}^{-9-j}).

(5.11)

Using Lemma
2.20
and (
2.13
), projecting (
5.10
) onto the ingoing
\ell=2
mode thus gives, in view of (
5.7
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}T^{j}(\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell=2}=T^{j}\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)\sum_{|m|\leq 2}P_{m}(r)Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.12)

Then using the expression (
2.29
) of the operator
\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}
, as well as (
2.10
), (
5.12
) rewrites

\displaystyle\sum_{|m|\leq 2}\Big[\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r^{2}}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})+(2iam+

\displaystyle 6(r-M))\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})\Big]Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)\sum_{|m|\leq 2}P_{m}(r)Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-\delta})

which, after projecting onto each
(\ell=2,m)
mode, concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

5.1.1
Resolution of the ODE (
5.6
)

In the setting of Proposition
5.1
, let us fix
{\underline{u}}\geq 1
,
|m|\leq 2
,
j\geq 0
. By Proposition
5.1
, the ODE satisfied by
{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}
(which is defined in (
5.5
)) is

\displaystyle\Delta{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime\prime}(r)+(2iam+6(r-M)){}^{(j)}\!f_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)=S_{m}^{(j)}(r,{\underline{u}}),

(5.13)

where the source
S_{m}^{(j)}(r,{\underline{u}})
satisfies

\displaystyle S_{m}^{(j)}(r,{\underline{u}})=P_{m}(r)\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.14)

We will determine
{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}
using the variation of constants method. To this end, we look for a basis of solutions to the homogeneous problem

\displaystyle\Delta v^{\prime\prime}(r)+(2iam+6(r-M))v^{\prime}(r)=0.

(5.15)

An obvious solution to (
5.15
) is

\displaystyle{}^{(2)}\!v_{1}(r):=1.

(5.16)

Another solution
{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}
satisfies

{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}^{\prime\prime}=-\frac{2iam+6(r-M)}{\Delta}{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}

thus up to a multiplicative constant,

{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}=\exp\left(\int^{r}-\frac{2iam+6(r^{\prime}-M)}{\Delta(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)=\Delta(r)^{-3}e^{-2imr_{mod}},

so that
({}^{(2)}\!v_{1},{}^{(2)}\!v_{2})
is a basis of solutions to (
5.15
), with

\displaystyle{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r):=\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta(r^{\prime})^{-3}e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.17)

We now state a result on the behavior of
{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r)
near
r=r_{\pm}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r)
be given by (
5.17
). We have the following asymptotic behaviors:

•

For
r_{\mathfrak{b}}\leq r\leq r_{+}
:

{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r)=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{4(r_{+}-M)+2iam}+O((r_{+}-r)^{-1}).

•

For
r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}
:

{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r)=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{4(r_{-}-M)+2iam}+O((r-r_{-})^{-1}).

Proof.

We begin with the first statement with the case
m\neq 0
. Recalling

\Delta^{\prime}(r)=2(r-M),\quad r_{mod}^{\prime}(r)=\frac{a}{\Delta},

we have the following integration by parts

\displaystyle\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2iam}\Bigg(\Bigg[\Delta^{-2}e^{-2imr_{mod}}\Bigg]_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}+\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}4(r^{\prime}-M)\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{2iam}-\frac{2(r-M)}{iam}\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad+O((r_{+}-r)^{-1}).

This yields, as stated,

\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{4(r_{+}-M)+2iam}+O((r_{+}-r)^{-1}).

The case
m=0
can be treated in an easier way, using
\Delta^{-3}\sim(r-r_{+})^{-3}
in
\mathbf{I}
and directly integrating. The second statement is proven by exchanging the roles of
r_{+}
and
r_{-}
.
∎

Using the basis
({}^{(2)}\!v_{1},{}^{(2)}\!v_{2})
of solutions of the homogeneous problem (
5.15
), we now use the variation of constants method to find a particular solution
{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}
of (
5.13
) as

{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r)={}^{(j)}\!M_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r){}^{(2)}\!v_{1}(r)+{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r){}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r),

where
{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r),{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r)
are functions such that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!v_{1}+{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}=0,\\
{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}+{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}=\Delta^{-1}S_{m}^{(j)}(r,{\underline{u}}).\end{cases}

(5.18)

Solving (
5.18
) gives, in view of (
5.16
), (
5.17
),

{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)=\Delta^{2}S_{m}^{(j)}(r,{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}},\quad{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)=-\Delta^{2}S_{m}^{(j)}(r,{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}}{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r).

Using Lemma
5.3
we get that
{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)
converges as
r\to r_{+}
. Thus our particular solution of (
5.13
) can be chosen as

{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r)=-\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r^{\prime})S_{m}^{(j)}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})S_{m}^{(j)}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

Using the definition of
{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r)
we get more precisely

\displaystyle{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r)=\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}S_{m}^{(j)}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})\left(\int_{r^{\prime}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.19)

Now, since
{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}-{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}
satisfies the homogeneous equation (
5.15
), there are constants
A_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})
and
B_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})
that depend only on
{\underline{u}}
,
m
and
j
, such that for any
r\in[r_{\mathfrak{b}},r_{+}]
,
{\underline{u}}\geq 1
,
|m|\leq 2
, and
j\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r)=A_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})+B_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}}){}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r)+{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r).

(5.20)

5.1.2
Computation of
A_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})
,
B_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})

Notice that
{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,2,{\underline{u}}}(r_{+})=0
and using Lemma
5.3
we have that
{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}
diverges as
r\to r_{+}
, thus using the smoothness of
\psi_{-2}
at
\mathcal{H}_{+}
yields necessarily

B_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})=0.

Next, evaluating (
5.20
) at
r=r_{+}
and using (
5.5
) and the initial assumption (
3.1
) on
\mathcal{H}_{+}
implies

A_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})={\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r_{+},{\underline{u}})}=O({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

Putting back into (
5.20
) the expression (
5.14
) of the source
S_{m}^{(j)}(r,{\underline{u}})
, we find in
\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}P_{m}(r^{\prime})\left(\int_{r^{\prime}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta})+O({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta})

\displaystyle=O({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

(5.21)

Remark 5.4
.

Recall that we expect an asymptotic in
u^{-8}
for
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
. The asymptotic behavior (
5.21
) in
\mathbf{I}
might seem too imprecise at first, but this imprecision will actually be canceled when passing from
\psi_{-2}
close to
\mathcal{H}_{+}
to
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
close to
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
. What will really influence the final asymptotic at
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
is actually the precise asymptotic in
{\underline{u}}^{-8}
of the radial derivative
\partial_{r}F_{m,2}(r,{\underline{u}})
in
\mathbf{I}
, that we obtain below.

Note that (
5.20
), together with
B_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})=0
and (
5.19
), yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})=A_{m,2}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})+\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}S_{m}^{(j)}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})\left(\int_{r^{\prime}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime},

thus we have in
\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})=\frac{1}{\Delta^{3}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}}}\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}S_{m}^{(j)}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

Re-injecting the definition (
5.14
) of
S_{m}^{(j)}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})
gives in
\mathbf{I}
the following precise asymptotic behavior

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})=\frac{1}{\Delta^{3}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}}}\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}P_{m}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.22)

We now propagate these asymptotics to
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}=\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
by a similar method.

5.2
Precise asymptotics of
(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=2}
in region
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}

We follow in region
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}=\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
a procedure analogous to the one in region
\mathbf{I}
of Section
5.1
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). Then the functions
G_{m,2}(r,u)
, defined in (
5.4
), satisfy in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
the following equations, for
|m|\leq 2
:

\displaystyle\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}G_{m,2}}{\partial r^{2}}\!(r,u)-(2iam+2(r-M))\frac{\partial G_{m,2}}{\partial r}\!(r,u)-4G_{m,2}(r,u)=\frac{\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}P_{m}(r)}{(2r^{*}-u)^{8}}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.23)

Proof of Proposition
5.5
.

Recall that in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
we have

\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}],

where
|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}
, see Propositions
4.11
and
4.21
, as well as Lemma
2.21
. Using (
2.33
), this gives the estimate in region
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
:

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{t}]}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2}.

(5.24)

As before, the Teukolsky equation writes, using the
(\partial_{t},\partial_{r})
decomposition of
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}
and (
5.24
),

\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=-\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

Using the identity
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}(\Delta^{2}\>\cdot\>)=\Delta^{2}\mathbf{T}_{-2}(\>\cdot\>)
, see (
2.21
), and (
5.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]=\frac{14\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}(5r+2ia\cos\theta+iam)Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.25)

Just like in (
5.12
), projecting onto the outgoing
\ell=2
mode thus gives, using
\phi_{+}=\phi_{-}+2r_{mod}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}(\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell=2}=\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}P_{m}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{-}}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}),

(5.26)

where we used Lemma
2.20
, (
2.13
), and (
5.7
). Then using the expression (
2.32
) of the operator
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}
, as well as (
2.9
), (
5.26
) rewrites

\displaystyle\sum_{|m|\leq 2}\Big[\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}G_{m,2}}{\partial r^{2}}\!(r,u)-(2iam+

\displaystyle 2(r-M))\frac{\partial G_{m,2}}{\partial r}\!(r,u)-4G_{m,2}(r,u)\Big]Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{-}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}P_{m}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{-}}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}),

which concludes the proof after projecting on each
(\ell=2,m)
mode and using
{\underline{u}}=2r^{*}-u
.
∎

5.2.1
Resolution of the ODE (
5.23
)

In the setting of Proposition
5.5
, let us consider values of
u\ll-1
such that the corresponding level sets are in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
. Recall the definition of
G_{m,2}
in (
5.5
), which satisfies the ODE

\displaystyle\Delta G_{m,2}^{\prime\prime}(r)-(2iam+2(r-M))G_{m,2}^{\prime}(r)-4G_{m,2}(r)=\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u),

(5.27)

where the source
\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u)
satisfies

\displaystyle\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u)=\frac{\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}P_{m}(r)}{(2r^{*}-u)^{8}}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.28)

As before, we will determine
G_{m,2}
using the variation of constants method. To this end, we look for a basis of solutions to the homogeneous problem

\displaystyle\Delta\widehat{v}^{\prime\prime}(r)-(2iam+2(r-M))\widehat{v}^{\prime}(r)-4\widehat{v}(r)=0,

(5.29)

using the variation of constants method, and the initial data on
\{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
. To solve (
5.29
), notice that if
v
satisfies (
5.15
) then
\widehat{v}:=v\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}
satisfies (
5.29
). Indeed, under these assumptions we have

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=[4(r-M)\Delta v+\Delta^{2}v^{\prime}+2iam\Delta v]e^{2imr_{mod}}

\displaystyle=[\Delta(4(r-M)+2iam)v+\Delta^{2}v^{\prime}]e^{2imr_{mod}},

and

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=[2(r-M)(4(r-M)+2iam)v+4\Delta v+\Delta(4(r-M)+2iam)v^{\prime}+4(r-M)\Delta v^{\prime}+\Delta^{2}v^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2iam(4(r-M)+2iam)v+2iam\Delta v^{\prime}]e^{2imr_{mod}},

\displaystyle=[2(r-M)+2iam]\Delta v^{\prime}e^{2imr_{mod}}+[(2(r-M)+2iam)(4(r-M)+2iam)+4\Delta]ve^{2imr_{mod}}

by (
5.15
), which gives as stated

\displaystyle\Delta\widehat{v}^{\prime\prime}=(2(r-M)+2iam)\widehat{v}^{\prime}+4\Delta^{2}ve^{2imr_{mod}}=(2(r-M)+2iam)\widehat{v}^{\prime}+4\widehat{v}.

Recalling the basis
({}^{(2)}v_{1},{}^{(2)}v_{2})
of solutions of (
5.15
), this yields a basis of solutions of (
5.29
) given by

\displaystyle{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r):=\Delta^{2}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}(r)},

(5.30)

\displaystyle{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r):=\Delta^{2}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}(r)}\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.31)

We now use the variation of constants method to find a particular solution

\widehat{p}_{m,2,u}(r):=\widehat{M}_{m,2,u}(r){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)+\widehat{N}_{m,2,u}(r){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)

of (
5.27
), where
\widehat{M}_{m,2,u}(r)
and
\widehat{N}_{m,2,u}(r)
are functions such that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\widehat{M}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}+\widehat{N}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}=0,\\
\widehat{M}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}+\widehat{N}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}={\Delta^{-1}}{\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u)}.\end{cases}

(5.32)

To solve (
5.32
), let us compute the Wronskian
\widehat{W}_{2}
of
({}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}},{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}})
. We have the identity

{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}={}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\frac{\Delta(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r^{\prime})^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime},

thus the Wronskian is

\widehat{W}_{2}={}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}-{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}=-\Delta e^{2imr_{mod}}.

This gives
\widehat{M}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}\widehat{W}_{2}=\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}/\Delta
and
\widehat{N}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}\widehat{W}_{2}=-\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}/\Delta
, hence

\widehat{M}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}(r)=\frac{-{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u)}{\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}},\quad\widehat{N}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}(r)=\frac{{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u)}{\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}}.

Re-injecting the definitions of
{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)
and
{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)
, this gives

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}(r)=-{}^{(2)}\!v_{2}(r)\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u)=-\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime},

\displaystyle\widehat{N}_{m,2,u}^{\prime}(r)=\widehat{S}_{m}(r,u).

Our particular solution can thus be chosen as

\displaystyle\widehat{p}_{m,2,u}(r)=-{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\widehat{S}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\left(\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r^{\prime}}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\widehat{S}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

We conclude that there are constants
\widehat{A}_{m,2}(u)
,
\widehat{B}_{m,2}(u)
such that in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
,

\displaystyle G_{m,2}(r,u)=

\displaystyle-{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\widehat{S}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\left(\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r^{\prime}}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\widehat{S}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\widehat{A}_{m,2}(u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)+\widehat{B}_{m,2}(u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r).

(5.33)

5.2.2
Computation of the constants
\widehat{A}_{m,2}(u)
,
\widehat{B}_{m,2}(u)

Evaluating (
5.33
) on
r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}
and using (
5.3
), we get

\widehat{A}_{m,2}(u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=G_{m,2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)=\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}F_{m,2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)),

where
{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)=2r_{\mathfrak{b}}^{*}-u
, which yields

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{m,2}(u)=F_{m,2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))=O(|u|^{-7-\delta}).

(5.34)

We also have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial G_{m,2}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\>\Big|_{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}F_{m,2}(r,2r^{*}-u))

\displaystyle=(4(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M)+2iam)\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}F_{m,2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))

\displaystyle\quad+2(r_{\mathfrak{b}}^{2}+a^{2})\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\frac{\partial F_{m,2}}{\partial{\underline{u}}}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))+\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\frac{\partial F_{m,2}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)).

Moreover, by (
5.33
) and using
{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=0
, we obtain

\frac{\partial G_{m,2}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)=\widehat{A}_{m,2}(u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})+\widehat{B}_{m,2}(u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}}).

Thus, using (
5.34
), (
5.21
) with
j=1
, as well as
{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=(4(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M)+2iam)\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}
, yields

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{m,2}(u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\frac{\partial F_{m,2}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

Using
{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=\Delta^{-1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})
, as well as (
5.22
) we get

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{m,2}(u)=\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)^{8}}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}P_{m}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O({\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)^{-8-\delta/2}).

Recalling
{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)=2r_{\mathfrak{b}}^{*}-u
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{m,2}(u)=\frac{1}{u^{8}}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}P_{m}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.35)

5.2.3
Precise asymptotic near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}

We now turn to estimating the terms in the RHS of (
5.33
) approaching
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
. A simple double integration bound gives that the first term of the first line of the RHS of (
5.33
) is bounded by
-\Delta(r)^{-1}|{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)|\lesssim r-r_{-}
. We also have
|\widehat{A}_{m,2}(u){}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)|\lesssim(r-r_{-})^{2}
by (
5.34
). For the second term on the first line of the RHS of (
5.33
), we first write using (
5.28
),

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\widehat{S}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{u^{8}}\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}P_{m}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\left(\frac{1}{(u-2(r^{\prime})^{*})^{8}}-\frac{1}{u^{8}}\right)\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}P_{m}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right|+|u|^{-8-\delta/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{r}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{2(r^{\prime})^{*}\Delta(r^{\prime})^{2}}{|u|^{9}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+|u|^{-8-\delta/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2}.

Using this, as well as the value (
5.35
) of
\widehat{B}_{m,2}(u)
gives

\displaystyle G_{m,2}(r,u)=\frac{{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)}{u^{8}}\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}P_{m}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2})+O(r-r_{-}).

(5.36)

Next, notice that
{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)
converges on
r=r_{-}
. Indeed, using Lemma
5.3
we get, as
r\to r_{-}
,

\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{4(r_{-}-M)+2iam}+O((r-r_{-})^{-1}),

which proves

{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r_{-})=-\frac{1}{4(r_{-}-M)+2iam},

as well as
|{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)-{}^{(2)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r_{-})|\lesssim r-r_{-}
. Combining this with the definition (
5.7
) of
P_{m}(r)
finally gives, for
r\in[r_{-},r_{\mathfrak{b}}]
and
w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}
,

\displaystyle G_{m,2}(r,u)=\frac{1}{u^{8}}

\displaystyle\frac{7Q_{m,2}}{2(r_{-}-M)+iam}\int_{r_{-}}^{r_{+}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(5r^{\prime}+iam+2iab_{m,2}^{-2})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2})+O(r-r_{-}).

Finally, combining this with the identity

(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=2}=\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+(\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell=2}=\sum_{|m|\leq 2}G_{m,2}(r,u)Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{-}}+O(r-r_{-})

concludes the proof of Theorem
3.5
.

Remark 5.6
.

The
O({\underline{u}}^{-7-\delta})
imprecise upper bound for
\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]
in
\mathbf{I}
does not appear anymore in the asymptotics of
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
, because it gets canceled by factors
r-r_{-}
when rescaling from
\psi_{-2}
to
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
. This allows us to deduce a precise
u^{-8}
asymptotic behavior for
\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
even though the precise asymptotic behavior of
\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]
is unknown on
\mathcal{H_{+}}
.

5.3
Upper bound for
(\psi_{-2})_{\ell=3}
in region
\mathbf{I}

We now prove Theorem
3.6
. We proceed exactly like for the modes
\ell=2
. The only difference will be the vanishing of the coefficient in front of
1/u^{8}
in the leading-order term of
(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}
. Recall the definition (
5.1
) of the functions
F_{m,3}(r,{\underline{u}})
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). Then, defining by convention
Q_{\pm 3,2}=0
, the functions
{F_{m,3}}(r,{\underline{u}})
satisfy in
\mathbf{I}
the equations, for
|m|\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r^{2}}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})+(2iam+6(r-M))\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})-6\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle=28ia\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.37)

Proof.

Using (
3.2
) we have in
\mathbf{I}
,
|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\leq 1}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}
. As
(\psi_{-2})_{\ell\geq 3}=(\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell\geq 3}
, we deduce the bounds:

\displaystyle|(\sin^{2}\theta T^{j+2}\psi_{-2})_{\ell=3}|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-9-j},

(5.38)

\displaystyle|(T^{j+1}\psi_{-2})_{\ell=3}|=|(T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell=3}|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta},

(5.39)

\displaystyle|(T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\psi_{-2})_{\ell=3}|=|(T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell=3}|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta},

(5.40)

\displaystyle|(T^{j+1}\Phi\psi_{-2})_{\ell=3}|=|(T^{j+1}\Phi\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell=3}|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}.

(5.41)

Using Proposition
2.7
we also have

\displaystyle(\cos\theta T^{j+1}\psi_{-2})_{\ell=3}=-\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{7}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)\sum_{|m|\leq 2}b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,3}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.42)

Moreover, the Teukolsky equation writes, using the
(\partial_{t},\partial_{r})
decomposition of the Teukolsky operator,

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}T^{j}\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{t}]}T^{j}\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle=-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{j+2}\psi_{-2}-2(r^{2}+a^{2})T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\psi_{-2}-2aT^{j+1}\Phi\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle\quad-(10r+4ia\cos\theta)T^{j+1}\psi_{-2}.

Projecting onto the
\ell=3
modes thus gives, using (
5.38
)–(
5.42
), (
2.13
) and Lemma
2.20
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}(T^{j}\psi_{-2})_{\ell=3}=28ia\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)\sum_{|m|\leq 2}b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,3}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.43)

Then using the expression (
2.29
) of the operator
\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}
, as well as (
2.10
), (
5.43
) rewrites

\displaystyle\sum_{|m|\leq 3}\Big[\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r^{2}}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})+(2iam+

\displaystyle 6(r-M))\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})-6\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})\Big]Y_{m,3}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}

\displaystyle=28ia\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)\sum_{|m|\leq 2}b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,3}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}),

which concludes the proof by projecting onto an ingoing
(\ell=3,m)
mode with
|m|\leq 3
.
∎

5.3.1
Resolution of the ODE (
5.37
)

In the setting of Proposition
5.7
, let us fix
{\underline{u}}\geq 1
and
j\geq 0
. Recall the functions
{}^{(j)}f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}
defined in (
5.5
). By (
5.37
), the ODE satisfied by
{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}
is

\displaystyle\Delta{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime\prime}(r)+(2iam+6(r-M)){}^{(j)}\!f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)-6{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)=R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}}),

(5.44)

where the source
R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}})
satisfies
