\displaystyle R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}})=28ia\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\right)b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}+O({\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.45)

As before, we will recover
{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}
using a basis of solutions of the homogeneous problem

\displaystyle\Delta v^{\prime\prime}(r)+(2iam+6(r-M))v^{\prime}(r)-6v(r)=0,

(5.46)

using the variation of constants method. An obvious solution of (
5.46
) is

\displaystyle{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r):=r-M+\frac{iam}{3}.

(5.47)

Let
{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}
be another solution of (
5.46
), and define the Wronskian

W_{3}:={}^{(3)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}-{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}={}^{(3)}\!v_{2}-(r-M+aim/3){}^{(3)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}.

We obtain, differentiating
W_{1}
,

\displaystyle W^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle=-(r-M+aim/3){}^{(3)}\!v_{2}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{r-M+aim/3}{\Delta}((6(r-M)+2iam){}^{(3)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}-6{}^{(3)}\!v_{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{6(r-M)+2iam}{\Delta}W_{1}.

Thus, making an appropriate choice of free constant, we have

W_{1}=\exp\left(-\int^{r}\frac{6(r^{\prime}-M)+2iam}{\Delta(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)=-\Delta^{-3}e^{-2imr_{mod}}.

This gives

\left(\frac{{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}}{{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}}\right)^{\prime}=-\frac{W_{1}}{{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}^{2}}=\frac{\Delta^{-3}e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)^{2}}.

Thus we can choose the other member of the basis of solutions of (
5.46
) to be

\displaystyle{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r):=(r-M+aim/3)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.48)

Remark 5.8
.

Note that even for
m=0
where
{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)
vanishes at
r=M
, the RHS of (
5.48
) admits a limit as
r\to M
, and is thus well defined on
(r_{-},r_{+})
.

Now we use the variation of constants method to find a particular solution
{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}
of (
5.44
) of the form

{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r):={}^{(j)}\!M_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r){}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)+{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r){}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r),

where
{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)
and
{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)
satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{cases}{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}+{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}=0,\\
{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}+{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}={\Delta}^{-1}{R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}})},\end{cases}

(5.49)

Solving (
5.49
) gives
{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}W_{1}=R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}}){}^{(3)}\!v_{2}/\Delta
and
{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}W_{1}=-R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}}){}^{(3)}\!v_{1}/\Delta
, hence

{}^{(j)}\!M_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)=-\Delta^{2}{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r)R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}},\quad{}^{(j)}\!N_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)=\Delta^{2}{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}}.

Our particular solution can thus be chosen as

\displaystyle{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)=-

\displaystyle{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r^{\prime})R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.50)

Notice that a naive upper bound for
{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}
is
(r_{+}-r)^{-2}
as
r\to r_{+}
, thus the first integral on the RHS of (
5.50
) is well-defined. Note that using the expression (
5.48
) of
{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}
yields

\displaystyle{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)=

\displaystyle-{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)\int_{r}^{r_{+}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}}{{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime\prime})^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)\left(\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)\int_{r}^{r_{+}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}{}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

Thus defining the function

\displaystyle K_{m}(r,r^{\prime}):=\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\int_{r^{\prime}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}}{(r^{\prime\prime}-M+aim/3)^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime},

(5.51)

we obtain the expression

\displaystyle{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)=(r-M+aim/3)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})K_{m}(r,r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.52)

As
{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}-{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}
satisfies the homogeneous equation (
5.46
), there are constants
A_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})
and
B_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})
that depend only on
{\underline{u}}
,
m
and
j
such that for any
r\in[r_{\mathfrak{b}},r_{+}]
, any
|m|\leq 2
, and any
{\underline{u}}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)=A_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}}){}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)+B_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}}){}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r)+(r-M+aim/3)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})K_{m}(r,r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.53)

5.3.2
Computation of
A_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})
,
B_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})

To compute the constants
A_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})
,
B_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})
in terms of the initial data on
\mathcal{H}_{+}
, we first discuss the regularity at
r=r_{+}
of the different functions at play. We begin with the following result

Lemma 5.9
.

The function
{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r)
defined by (
5.48
) diverges as
r\to r_{\pm}
. More precisely,

•

For
r_{\mathfrak{b}}\leq r\leq r_{+}
:

{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r)=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{(4(r_{+}-M)+2iam)(r_{+}-M+aim/3)}+O((r_{+}-r)^{-1}).

•

For
r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}
:

{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r)=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{(4(r_{-}-M)+2iam)(r_{-}-M+aim/3)}+O((r-r_{-})^{-1}).

Proof.

We begin with the first point. We have the estimate

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}-\frac{1}{(r-M+aim/3)^{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{r}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(r_{+}-r^{\prime})^{-2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\lesssim(r_{+}-r)^{-1}.

We conclude using Lemma
5.3
. The second point is proved in a similar way.
∎

Now, we prove
{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)\to 0
as
r\to r_{+}
. Indeed, we have already seen that the first integral on the RHS of (
5.50
) is well-defined and thus goes to zero as
r\to r_{+}
. We can also easily bound the other term:

\left|{}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right|\lesssim(r_{+}-r)^{-2}\int_{r}^{r_{+}}(r_{+}-r^{\prime})^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\lesssim r_{+}-r,

which proves that indeed
{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)\to 0
as
r\to r_{+}
. This implies, by (
5.53
),

\left|{}^{(j)}\!f_{m,{\underline{u}}}(r)-A_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}}){}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)-B_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}}){}^{(3)}\!v_{2}(r)\right|\lesssim|{}^{(j)}\!p_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)|\underset{r\to r_{+}}{\longrightarrow}0.

As
T^{j}\psi_{-2}
is regular at
r=r_{+}
,
\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!F_{m,3}(r,{\underline{u}})={}^{(j)}\!f_{m,3,{\underline{u}}}(r)
is also regular at
r=r_{+}
. By Lemma
5.9
, this implies necessarily
B_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})=0
, and thus by (
5.53
),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})=A_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}}){}^{(3)}\!v_{1}(r)+(r-M+aim/3)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})K_{m}(r,r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.54)

Evaluating at
r=r_{+}
and using the initial assumption (
3.1
) on
\mathcal{H}_{+}
, we find

A_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})=(r_{+}-M+aim/3)^{-1}{\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r_{+},{\underline{u}})}=O({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

Putting back into (
5.53
) the expression (
5.45
) of the source
R^{(j)}_{m}(r,{\underline{u}})
, we get in
\mathbf{I}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})=O({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

(5.55)

Note that this bound is imprecise. Instead what we will use is the precise asymptotic of a linear combination of
\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})
and
\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})
. Differentiating (
5.54
) with respect to
r
we find in
\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\left(\frac{\partial^{j}F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}^{j}}\!\right)(r,{\underline{u}})=

\displaystyle\>A_{m,3}^{(j)}({\underline{u}})+\int_{r_{+}}^{r}R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})K_{m}(r,r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

(5.56)

\displaystyle+\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}(r)}}{r-M+aim/3}\int_{r_{+}}^{r}R^{(j)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}})\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

5.4
Upper bound for
(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}
in region
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}

We continue the analysis in region
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}=\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
.

Proposition 5.10
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). Then, defining by convention
Q_{\pm 3,2}=0
, the functions
{G_{m,3}(r,u)}
satisfy the following equations in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
, for
|m|\leq 3
:

\displaystyle\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}G_{m,3}}{\partial r^{2}}\!(r,u)-(2iam+2(r-M))\frac{\partial G_{m,3}}{\partial r}\!

\displaystyle(r,u)-10{G_{m,3}(r,u)}=

\displaystyle\frac{28ia\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.57)

Proof.

Recall that in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}=\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
, we have

\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}],

where
|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}
, using Propositions
4.11
and
4.21
, as well as Lemma
2.21
. This yields the estimates in region
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
:

\displaystyle|(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}|\lesssim|u|^{-9},

(5.58)

\displaystyle|(T\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}|=|(T\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell=3}|\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2},

(5.59)

\displaystyle|(T\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}|=|(T\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell=3}|\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2},

(5.60)

\displaystyle|(T\Phi\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}|=|(T\Phi\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell=3}|\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2}.

(5.61)

Moreover, by (
5.42
) with
j=0
we get

\displaystyle(\cos\theta T\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}=-\frac{7\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,3}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.62)

The Teukolsky equation writes, using the
(\partial_{t},\partial_{r})
decomposition (
2.28
) of
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}
,

\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=-\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{t}]}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}+2(r^{2}+a^{2})T\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}-2aT\Phi\widehat{\psi}_{-2}-(6r+4ia\cos\theta)T\psi_{-2}.

Projecting onto the modes
\ell=3
thus gives, using (
5.58
)–(
5.62
) and (
2.13
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}=\frac{28ia\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,3}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.63)

Then, using the expression (
2.32
) of the operator
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}
, as well as (
2.9
), (
5.63
) rewrites

\displaystyle\sum_{|m|\leq 3}\Big[\Delta\partial_{r}^{2}{G_{m,3}(r,u)}-(2iam+

\displaystyle 2(r-M))\partial_{r}{G_{m,3}(r,u)}-10{G_{m,3}(r,u)}\Big]Y_{m,3}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{-}}

\displaystyle=\frac{28ia\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{8}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,3}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}),

which concludes the proof, projecting on each outgoing
(\ell=3,m)
mode with
|m|\leq 3
.
∎

5.4.1
Resolution of the ODE (
5.57
)

In the setting of Proposition
5.10
, let us consider values of
u\ll-1
such that the corresponding level sets are in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
. Recall the function
g_{m,3,u}(r)={G_{m,3}(r,u)}
. By (
5.57
), the ODE satisfied by
g_{m,3,u}
is

\displaystyle\Delta g_{m,3,u}^{\prime\prime}(r)-(2iam+2(r-M))g_{m,3,u}^{\prime}(r)-10g_{m,3,u}(r)=\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u),

(5.64)

where the source
\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u)
satisfies

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u)=\frac{28ia\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}}{(2r^{*}-u)^{8}}b_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.65)

As before, we use a basis of solutions to the homogeneous problem

\displaystyle\Delta\widehat{v}^{\prime\prime}(r)-(2iam+2(r-M))\widehat{v}^{\prime}(r)-10\widehat{v}(r)=0,

(5.66)

To solve (
5.66
), notice that if
v
satisfies (
5.46
), then
\widehat{v}:=v\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}
satisfies (
5.66
). Indeed, under these assumptions we have as before

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=[\Delta(4(r-M)+2iam)v+\Delta^{2}v^{\prime}]e^{2imr_{mod}},

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=[2(r-M)(4(r-M)+2iam)v+4\Delta v+\Delta(4(r-M)+2iam)v^{\prime}+4(r-M)\Delta v^{\prime}+\Delta^{2}v^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2iam(4(r-M)+2iam)v+2iam\Delta v^{\prime}]e^{2imr_{mod}},

\displaystyle=[2(r-M)+2iam]\Delta v^{\prime}e^{2imr_{mod}}+[(2(r-M)+2iam)(4(r-M)+2iam)+10\Delta]ve^{2imr_{mod}},

which gives as stated

\displaystyle\Delta\widehat{v}^{\prime\prime}=(2(r-M)+2iam)\widehat{v}^{\prime}+10\Delta^{2}ve^{2imr_{mod}}=(2(r-M)+2iam)\widehat{v}^{\prime}+10\widehat{v}.

This proves, recalling (
5.47
), (
5.48
), that a basis
({}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}},{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}})
of solutions of (
5.66
) can be chosen as

\displaystyle{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r):=(r-M+{iam}/{3})\Delta^{2}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}(r)},

(5.67)

\displaystyle{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r):=(r-M+aim/3)\Delta^{2}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}(r)}\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.68)

Now we do the variation of constants method to find a particular solution
\widehat{p}_{m,3,u}(r)
of (
5.64
). As before, let
\widehat{M}_{m,3,u}(r)
,
\widehat{N}_{m,3,u}(r)
be functions such that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\widehat{M}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}+\widehat{N}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}=0,\\
\widehat{M}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}+\widehat{N}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}={\Delta}^{-1}{\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u)},\end{cases}

(5.69)

then
\widehat{p}_{m,3,u}=\widehat{M}_{m,3,u}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}+\widehat{N}_{m,3,u}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}
is a solution of (
5.64
). To solve (
5.69
), let us compute the Wronskian
\widehat{W}_{3}
of
({}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}},{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}})
. We have

{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}={}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\frac{\Delta(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r^{\prime})^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

thus the Wronskian is

\widehat{W}_{3}={}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}-{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}=-\Delta e^{2imr_{mod}}.

This gives
\widehat{M}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}\widehat{W}_{3}=\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}/\Delta
and
\widehat{N}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}\widehat{W}_{3}=-\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}/\Delta
, hence

\widehat{M}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}=\frac{-{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u)}{\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}},\quad\widehat{N}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}=\frac{{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u)}{\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}}.

More precisely, this gives

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}=-{}^{(3)}\!{v_{2}}(r)\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u)=-(r-M+aim/3)\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime},

\displaystyle\widehat{N}_{m,3,u}^{\prime}={}^{(3)}\!{v_{1}}(r)\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u)=(r-M+aim/3)\widehat{R}_{m}(r,u).

Our particular solution can thus be chosen as

\displaystyle\widehat{p}_{m,3,u}(r)=

\displaystyle-{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\widehat{R}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\left(\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}}{(r^{\prime\prime}-M+aim/3)^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\widehat{R}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

Finally, we get that there are constants
\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u)
,
\widehat{B}_{m,3}(u)
such that in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
we have

\displaystyle{G_{m,3}(r,u)}=

\displaystyle-{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\widehat{R}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\left(\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime\prime})}}{(r^{\prime\prime}-M+aim/3)^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\widehat{R}_{m}(r^{\prime},u)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)+\widehat{B}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r).

(5.70)

5.4.2
Computation of the constants
\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u)
,
\widehat{B}_{m,3}(u)

We have by (
5.70
),

\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=G_{m,3}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)=\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{F_{m,3}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))

which yields

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{{F_{m,3}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))}{r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M+aim/3}.

(5.71)

We also have, using (
5.3
),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial G_{m,3}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!\>\Big|_{r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}{F_{m,3}}(r,2r^{*}-u))

\displaystyle=(4(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M)+2iam)\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{F_{m,3}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))

\displaystyle\quad+2(r_{\mathfrak{b}}^{2}+a^{2})\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\frac{\partial F_{m,3}}{\partial{\underline{u}}}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))+\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\frac{\partial F_{m,3}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)).

Moreover, by (
5.70
), using
{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=0
,

\frac{\partial G_{m,3}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)=\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})+\widehat{B}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}}),

thus using (
5.55
) with
j=1
, this gives

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})+\widehat{B}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=

\displaystyle(4(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M)+2iam)\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{F_{m,3}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\partial_{r}{F_{m,3}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

Moreover, we have by (
5.71
),

\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=\Bigg[(4(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M)+2iam)\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}+\frac{\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}}{r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M+aim/3}\Bigg]{F_{m,3}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)),

thus using (
5.56
) and (
5.54
) (i.e. the special combination of
\partial_{r}{F_{m,3}}
and
{F_{m,3}}
which cancels the term with
A_{m,3}({\underline{u}})
), we get

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})

\displaystyle=\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\Bigg[\frac{\partial F_{m,3}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))-\frac{{F_{m,3}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))}{r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M+aim/3}\Bigg]+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta^{-1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M+aim/3}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}R^{(0)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

Finally, using
{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=\Delta^{-1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})/(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M+aim/3)
, (
5.45
) with
j=0
, and
{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)=2r_{\mathfrak{b}}^{*}-u
, we find

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{m,3}(u)

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}R^{(0)}_{m}(r^{\prime},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{28aib_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}}{u^{8}}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.72)

5.4.3
More precise estimate near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}

We now turn to estimating the terms on the RHS of (
5.70
) near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
. A simple double integration bound gives that the first line is bounded by
-\Delta(r)^{-1}|{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)|\lesssim r-r_{-}
. We also have
|\widehat{A}_{m,3}(u){}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)|\lesssim(r-r_{-})^{2}
. For the term on the second line of the RHS of (
5.70
), we have using (
5.65
),

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\widehat{R}_{m}(r^{\prime},

\displaystyle u)(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}-\frac{28aimb_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}}{u^{8}}\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\left(\frac{1}{(u-2(r^{\prime})^{*})^{8}}-\frac{1}{u^{8}}\right)\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right|+|u|^{-8-\delta/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{r}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{2(r^{\prime})^{*}\Delta(r^{\prime})^{2}}{|u|^{9}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+|u|^{-8-\delta/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2}.

Next, notice that
{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)
converges on
r=r_{-}
. Indeed, using Lemma
5.9
, we find

{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\underset{r\to r_{-}}{\longrightarrow}-\frac{1}{(4(r_{-}-M)+2iam)(r_{-}-M+aim/3)},

and
|{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)-{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r_{-})|\lesssim r-r_{-}
. Finally, taking into account the expression (
5.72
) of
\widehat{B}_{m,3}(u)
, we get

\displaystyle{G_{m,3}(r,u)}=

\displaystyle\frac{28aib_{m,2}^{-2}Q_{m,2}{}^{(3)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r_{-})}{u^{8}}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{-}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad+O(|u|^{-8-\delta/2})+O(r-r_{-}).

Finally, notice that

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{-}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}(r^{\prime}-M+aim/3)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}=\frac{1}{6}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{-}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}}\Big[\Delta^{3}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\Big]\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}=0,

(5.73)

which gives
|G_{m,3}(r,u)|\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2}+r-r_{-}
and thus, by (
5.4
), concludes the proof of Theorem
3.6
.

Remark 5.11
.

We saw that for
(\psi_{-2})_{\ell=2}
, which decays like
{\underline{u}}^{-7}
on
\mathcal{H_{+}}
, the constant in front of
1/u^{7}
in the asymptotic development of
(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=2}
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
vanishes. Strikingly, this is also the case for
\ell=3
where
(\psi_{-2})_{\ell=3}
is expected to decay like
{\underline{u}}^{-8}
on
\mathcal{H_{+}}

[
25
, Section 1.3, (2)]
, while the constant in front of
1/u^{8}
in the asymptotic development of
(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell=3}
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
vanishes by (
5.73
). We expect that this cancellation occurs for every fixed
\ell
mode of
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
.

5.5
Upper bound for
(\psi_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}
in region
\mathbf{I}

The rest of this section is dedicated to proving Theorem
3.7
. We proceed exactly like for the modes
\ell=2
and
\ell=3
, but this time the source terms of the ODEs will be directly treated as an error term. Here we consider
k\in\{0,1\}
,
j\in\{0,1,2\}
, and
18
18

18

The ODE method will give control of
\|(\widehat{\psi})_{\ell\geq 4}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}
near
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
, and we will recover a pointwise bound for
|(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}|
by the Sobolev embedding (
2.19
).

\psi:=\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\psi_{-2},\quad\widehat{\psi}:=\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}.

We will consider the spin-weighted spherical harmonics decomposition

\displaystyle(\psi)_{\ell\geq 4}=\sum_{\ell\geq 4}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}F_{m,\ell}(r,{\underline{u}})Y_{m,\ell}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}},\quad(\widehat{\psi})_{\ell\geq 4}=\sum_{\ell\geq 4}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}G_{m,\ell}(r,u)Y_{m,\ell}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{-}},

(5.74)

such that
G_{m,\ell}(r,u)=F_{m,\ell}(r,2r^{*}-u)\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}
.

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
\ell\geq 4
. Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). Then, the functions
{F_{m,\ell}}(r,{\underline{u}})
satisfy in
\mathbf{I}
the equations, for
|m|\leq\ell
,

\displaystyle\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}F_{m,\ell}}{\partial r^{2}}\!(r,{\underline{u}})+(2iam+6(r-M))\frac{\partial F_{m,\ell}}{\partial r}\!(r,{\underline{u}})+\lambda_{\ell}{F_{m,\ell}}(r,{\underline{u}})=O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}),

(5.75)

where
\lambda_{\ell}=-(\ell-2)(\ell+3)
.

Proof.

Using (
3.2
) we have in
\mathbf{I}

\psi=\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\left(\frac{1}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)+\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}],

where
|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\leq 1}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]|\lesssim{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}
. By Proposition
2.7
, we get for
k=0,1
, and for
\ell\geq 4
,

\displaystyle(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\leq 1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\psi_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}^{+}=(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\leq 1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{(m,\ell)}^{+}+O({\underline{u}}^{-9-j}),

where the term
O({\underline{u}}^{-9-j})
on the RHS is only non-zero for
\ell=4
, so we can express it as
O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})
. Thus by Proposition
2.12
we deduce the following bounds in
\mathbf{I}
, for
|m|\leq\ell
:

\displaystyle|(\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+2}\psi_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{+}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq 2}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{j+2}\psi_{-2})\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}\lesssim\ell^{-2}{{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}},

(5.76)

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\psi_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{+}|

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell,m}^{+}|+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})

(5.77)

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq 2}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}\lesssim\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta},

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\psi_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{+}|

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell,m}^{+}|+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})

(5.78)

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq 2}T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}\lesssim\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta},

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\Phi\psi_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{+}|

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\Phi\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell,m}^{+}|+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})

(5.79)

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq 2}T^{j+1}\Phi\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}]\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}\lesssim\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}.

Using Proposition
2.7
again we also get

\displaystyle|(\cos\theta\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\psi_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{+}|

\displaystyle=|(\cos\theta T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])_{\ell,m}^{+}|+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq 2}(\cos\theta T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\psi_{-2}])\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}.

(5.80)

Moreover, the Teukolsky equation writes, using the
(\partial_{t},\partial_{r})
decomposition of the Teukolsky operator,

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{t}]}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle=-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+2}\psi_{-2}-2(r^{2}+a^{2})\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\psi_{-2}-2a\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\Phi\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle\quad-(10r+4ia\cos\theta)\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\psi_{-2}.

Projecting onto the ingoing
(\ell,m)
mode thus gives, using (
5.76
)–(
5.80
),

\displaystyle(\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\psi_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{+}=O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}).

(5.81)

Then using the expression (
2.29
) of the operator
\mathbf{T}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}
, as well as (
2.10
) and Lemma
2.20
, (
5.81
) rewrites as (
5.75
) for
|m|\leq\ell
, as stated.
∎

Remark 5.13
.

We lose two angular derivatives here to obtain a factor
\ell^{-2}
in RHS of (
5.75
). This is done in order to avoid issues of summability with respect to
\ell
, see (
5.109
).

5.5.1
Resolution of the ODE (
5.75
)

We fix
\ell\geq 4
,
|m|\leq\ell
for the rest of the section. In the setting of Proposition
5.12
, let us fix
{\underline{u}}\geq 1
. By (
5.75
), the ODE satisfied by
f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}={F_{m,\ell}}(r,{\underline{u}})
is

\displaystyle\Delta f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime\prime}(r)+(2iam+6(r-M))f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\ell}f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r)=O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta}),

(5.82)

As before, we will recover
f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}
using a basis of solutions of the homogeneous problem

\displaystyle\Delta v^{\prime\prime}(r)+(2iam+6(r-M))v^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\ell}v(r)=0,

(5.83)

using the variation of constants method.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
\ell\geq 4
and
|m|\leq 4
. The function

\displaystyle{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r):=(r-r_{-})^{\ell-2}+\sum_{k=0}^{\ell-3}\left(\prod_{j=k}^{\ell-3}\frac{(j+1)((j+3)(r_{+}-r_{-})-2iam)}{j(j+5)-(\ell-2)(\ell+3)}\right)(r-r_{-})^{k}

(5.84)

satisfies the following:

1.

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)
is a solution of the ODE (
5.83
) on
[r_{-},r_{+}]
,

2.

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\neq 0
near

,

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(2M-r)=(-1)^{\ell-2}\overline{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)}
for
r\in[r_{-},r_{+}]
,

4.

|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)|\leq|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})|
for
r\in[r_{-},r_{+}]
.

Moreover, there exists a function
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)
defined on
(r_{-},r_{+})
which satisfies the following:

1.

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)
is a solution of the ODE (
5.83
) on
(r_{-},r_{+})
,

2.

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)
is linearly independent from
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)
,

3.

the Wronskian
W_{(m,\ell)}:={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}-{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}
satisfies

\displaystyle W_{(m,\ell)}=-\Delta^{-3}e^{-2imr_{mod}},

(5.85)

4.

the asymptotics of
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)
at
r\to r_{\pm}
are given as follows:

•

For
r_{\mathfrak{b}}\leq r\leq r_{+}
:

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{(4(r_{+}-M)+2iam){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{+})}+O_{(m,\ell)}((r_{+}-r)^{-1}).

•

For
r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}
:

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{(4(r_{-}-M)+2iam){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})}+O_{(m,\ell)}((r-r_{-})^{-1}),

where the implicit constants may depend on
(m,\ell)
.

Proof.

See Appendix
B
.

Remark 5.15
.

We remark the following :

1.

The bound in
4
is crucial to ensure that the bounds for
(\widehat{\psi})_{(m,\ell)}
are summable with respect to
\ell\geq 4
,
|m|\leq\ell
, see (
5.108
).

2.

With a bit more work, similarly as for the
\ell=3
case we could have defined
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)
by

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r):={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime},

but this will not be needed here.

Now that we have a basis
({}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1},{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2})
of solutions of (
5.83
), we use as before the variation of constants method to find a particular solution
p_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}
of (
5.82
) of the form

\displaystyle p_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r)=-

\displaystyle{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.86)

Notice that Proposition
5.14
,
4
ensures that the first integral on the RHS of (
5.86
) is well-defined. As
f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}-p_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}
satisfies the homogeneous equation (
5.83
), we get
A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}})
and
B_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}})
such that

\displaystyle f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r)=A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)+B_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)+p_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r).

(5.87)

5.5.2
Computation of
A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}})
,
B_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}})

We also have, as before,
p_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r)\to 0
as
r\to r_{+}
. This implies, by (
5.87
),

\left|f_{m,{\underline{u}}}(r)-A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)-B_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)\right|\lesssim|p_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r)|\underset{r\to r_{+}}{\longrightarrow}0.

As
\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\psi_{-2}
is regular at
r=r_{+}
,
F_{m,\ell}(r,{\underline{u}})=f_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r)
is also regular at
r=r_{+}
. By Proposition
5.14
,
4
, this implies necessarily
B_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}})=0
, and thus by (
5.87
),

\displaystyle{F_{m,\ell}}(r,{\underline{u}})=A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)+p_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}(r).

(5.88)

Evaluating at
r=r_{+}
and using Proposition
2.12
and the initial assumption (
3.1
) on
\mathcal{H}_{+}
, we find

A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}})={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{+})^{-1}{{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{+},{\underline{u}})}=O({}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{+})^{-1}\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}).

This yields, in
\mathbf{I}
,
{F_{m,\ell}}(r,{\underline{u}})=O_{(m,\ell)}\left({\underline{u}}^{-7-j-\delta}\right)
, near
r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}
. Once again, we will instead use a particular combination of
\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\!{F_{m,\ell}}(r,{\underline{u}})
and
{F_{m,\ell}}(r,{\underline{u}})
. Differentiating (
5.88
) with respect to

we find in
\mathbf{I}
, close to
