,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{F_{m,\ell}}}{\partial r}\!

\displaystyle=A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)+p_{m,\ell,{\underline{u}}}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)+{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle=A_{m,\ell}({\underline{u}}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)+\frac{\Delta^{-3}e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)}\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)}\left({}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime}){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}-[{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}\leftrightarrow{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}]\right),

where we used
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}-{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}=-\Delta^{-3}e^{-2imr_{mod}}
. Thus, by (
5.88
) and (
5.86
), near
r=r_{\mathfrak{b}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{F_{m,\ell}}}{\partial r}\!=\frac{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)}{F_{m,\ell}}+\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}(r)}}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)}\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-8-j-\delta})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

(5.89)

5.6
Upper bound for
(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}
in region
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}

We continue the analysis in region
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}=\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
.

Proposition 5.16
.

Assume that
\psi_{-2}
satisfies (
3.1
). Then the functions
{G_{m,\ell}(r,u)}
, defined in (
5.74
), satisfy in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
the following equations, for
|m|\leq\ell
:

\displaystyle\Delta\frac{\partial^{2}G_{m,\ell}}{\partial r^{2}}\!(r,u)-(2iam+2(r-M))\frac{\partial G_{m,\ell}}{\partial r}\!

\displaystyle(r,u)+\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime}{G_{m,\ell}(r,u)}=O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-\delta/2}),

(5.90)

where
\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell}=-(\ell+2)(\ell-1)=\lambda_{\ell}-4
.

Proof.

Recall that in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}=\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
, we have

\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{\underline{u}}^{7}}\sum_{|m|\leq 2}Q_{m,2}Y_{m,2}^{-2}(\cos\theta)e^{im\phi_{+}}\right)+\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}],

where
|T^{j}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|\lesssim|u|^{-7-j-\delta/2}
, using Propositions
4.11
and
4.21
, as well as Lemma
2.21
. By the bound

\displaystyle(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}^{-}=(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{(m,\ell)}^{-}+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}),

and Proposition
2.12
, this yields the following estimates in region
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}\cap\{w\leq w_{r_{\mathfrak{b}},\gamma}\}
:

\displaystyle|(\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{-}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq j+2}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}\lesssim\ell^{-2}|u|^{-9-j},

(5.91)

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{-}|

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell,m}^{-}|+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})

(5.92)

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq j+2}T\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}\lesssim\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2},

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{-}|

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell,m}^{-}|+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})

(5.93)

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq j+2}T\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}\lesssim\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2},

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\Phi\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{-}|

\displaystyle=|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\Phi\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell,m}^{-}|+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})

(5.94)

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq j+2}T\Phi\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]|+\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}\lesssim\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2}.

Moreover, using Proposition
2.7
again we get

\displaystyle|(\cos\theta\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{-}|

\displaystyle=|(\cos\theta\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{\ell,m}^{-}|+O(\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j})

(5.95)

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{\leq k+1}T^{\leq j+2}(\cos\theta T\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])\|_{L^{\infty}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+\ell^{-2}{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}\lesssim\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2}.

(5.96)

The Teukolsky equation writes, using the
(\partial_{t},\partial_{r})
decomposition (
2.28
) of
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}

\displaystyle=-\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{t}]}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}

\displaystyle=-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}+2(r^{2}+a^{2})\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}-2a\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\Phi\widehat{\psi}_{-2}

\displaystyle\quad-(6r+4ia\cos\theta)\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}.

Projecting onto an outgoing
(\ell,m)
mode thus gives, using (
5.91
)–(
5.95
),

\displaystyle(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell,m}^{-}=O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2}).

(5.97)

Then, using the expression (
2.32
) of the operator
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{-2}^{[\partial_{r}]}
, as well as (
2.9
) and Lemma
2.20
, (
5.97
) rewrites as (
5.90
) for
|m|\leq\ell
, as stated.
∎

Remark 5.17
.

Note that in order for the bounds (
5.91
)–(
5.95
) to hold by Propositions
4.11
and
4.21
, we need
N_{k}\geq 12
and
N_{j}\geq 10
. This is where we lose the maximum number of derivatives in this paper.

5.6.1
Resolution of the ODE (
5.90
)

In the setting of Proposition
5.16
, let us consider values of
u\ll-1
such that the corresponding level sets are in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
. By (
5.90
), the ODE satisfied by
g_{m,\ell,u}=G_{m,\ell}(r,u)
is

\displaystyle\Delta g_{m,\ell,u}^{\prime\prime}(r)-(2iam+2(r-M))g_{m,\ell,u}^{\prime}(r)+\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell}g_{m,\ell,u}(r)=O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-\delta/2}).

(5.98)

As before, we use a basis of solutions to the homogeneous problem

\displaystyle\Delta\widehat{v}^{\prime\prime}(r)-(2iam+2(r-M))\widehat{v}^{\prime}(r)+\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell}\widehat{v}(r)=0.

(5.99)

To solve (
5.99
), notice that as in the case
\ell=3
, if
v
satisfies (
5.83
), then
\widehat{v}:=v\Delta^{2}e^{2imr_{mod}}
satisfies (
5.99
). This proves that a basis
({}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}},{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}})
of solutions of (
5.99
) is

\displaystyle{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r):={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\Delta^{2}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}(r)},

(5.100)

\displaystyle{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r):={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)\Delta^{2}(r)e^{2imr_{mod}(r)}.

(5.101)

As before, by the variation of constants method, we find a particular solution
\widehat{p}_{m,\ell,u}(r)
of (
5.98
):

\displaystyle\widehat{p}_{m,\ell,u}(r)=

\displaystyle-{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle+{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime},

(5.102)

and we get constants
\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u)
,
\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}(u)
such that in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
we have

\displaystyle{G_{m,\ell}(r,u)}=\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)+\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)+\widehat{p}_{m,\ell,u}(r).

(5.103)

5.6.2
Computation of the constants
\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u)
,
\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}(u)

We have by (
5.103
),

\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=G_{m,\ell}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)=\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))

which yields

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}=O_{(m,\ell)}(1).

(5.104)

We also have as before,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial G_{m,\ell}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)

\displaystyle=(4(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M)+2iam)\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))

\displaystyle\quad+2(r_{\mathfrak{b}}^{2}+a^{2})\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\frac{\partial F_{m,\ell}}{\partial{\underline{u}}}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))+\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\frac{\partial F_{m,\ell}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)).

Moreover, by (
5.103
), using
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=0
,

\frac{\partial G_{m,\ell}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},u)=\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})+\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}}),

thus using
\partial_{\underline{u}}{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))=(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))
, this gives

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})+\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}

\displaystyle(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=(4(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M)+2iam)\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\partial_{r}{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))+O((\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))).

Moreover, we have by (
5.104
),

\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=\Bigg[(4(r_{\mathfrak{b}}-M)+2iam)\Delta(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}+\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\frac{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\Bigg]{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)),

thus using (
5.89
) and (
5.88
), we get

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})

\displaystyle=\Delta^{2}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})e^{2imr_{mod}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\Bigg[\frac{\partial F_{m,\ell}}{\partial r}\!(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))-\frac{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{F_{m,\ell}}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))\Bigg]+O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta^{-1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}(2(r^{\prime})^{*}-u)^{-8-j-\delta})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+O((\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))).

Finally, using
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})=\Delta^{-1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})/{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})
, we find

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}(u)

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}(2(r^{\prime})^{*}-u)^{-8-j-\delta})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+O({}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)))

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\Delta^{2}(r^{\prime})e^{2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+O({}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))).

(5.105)

We can now estimate the different terms in (
5.103
). First, by (
5.104
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{A}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{(m,\ell)}r-r_{-}.

(5.106)

Next, the first term in the RHS of (
5.102
) can be bounded using Proposition
5.14
,
4
, as

\displaystyle\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{1}}(r)\int_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}^{r}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right|\lesssim_{(m,\ell)}\Delta^{2}\int_{r}^{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}}{(r-r_{-})^{2}}+r-r_{-}\lesssim_{(m,\ell)}r-r_{-}.

(5.107)

Finally, the last term in the RHS of (
5.103
), which is the sum of
\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)
with the second line in (
5.102
), can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{}^{(m,\ell)}\!\widehat{v_{2}}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O({}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)))

\displaystyle=\frac{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})^{-1}}{(4(M-r_{-})-2iam)}\left(\int_{r_{+}}^{r}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O({}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)))\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+O_{(m,\ell)}(r-r_{-})

by the expression (
5.105
) of
\widehat{B}_{m,\ell}(u){}^{(m,\ell)}
, and by Proposition
5.14
,
4
. Finally, by Proposition
5.14
,
4
, we have the bound
|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})|/|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})|\leq 1
, thus

\displaystyle\left|\frac{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})^{-1}}{(4(M-r_{-})-2iam)}\left(\int_{r_{+}}^{r}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O({}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{\mathfrak{b}})(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)))\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2}+|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))|,

(5.108)

where the implicit constant in the bound above does not depend on
(m,\ell)
, and where we used

|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)))|\lesssim|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)))|+{\underline{u}}^{-9-j},

where the term
{\underline{u}}^{-9-j}
is non-zero only for
\ell=4
by Proposition
2.7
, as
k\in\{0,1\}
. Finally, combining (
5.103
), (
5.106
), (
5.107
), (
5.108
) yields the bound

G_{m,\ell}(r,u)=O(\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2})+O(|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))|)+O_{(m,\ell)}(r-r_{-}).

We deduce that on
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
,

|G_{m,\ell}(r_{-},u)|\lesssim\ell^{-2}|u|^{-8-j-\delta/2}+|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))|,

where the implicit constant does not depend on
(m,\ell)
, and thus by (
5.74
),

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,+\infty))}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell\geq 4}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}\Big(\ell^{-4}|u|^{-2(8+j+\delta/2)}+|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}])_{(m,\ell)}(r_{\mathfrak{b}},{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u))|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim|u|^{-2(8+j+\delta/2)}+\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}^{k}T^{j+1}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}_{r_{\mathfrak{b}}}(u)))}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|u|^{-2(8+j+\delta/2)}

(5.109)

on
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
, by (
3.2
). This yields, by the Sobolev embedding (
2.19
),

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}(r_{-},u,\theta,\phi_{-})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}T^{\leq 2}(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,+\infty))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mathcal{Q}_{-2}T^{\leq 2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,+\infty))}+|u|^{-8-\delta/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2}

(5.110)

on
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
. Note that here we used for the second bound the computation on
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
(where
\widehat{\psi}_{-2}=\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]
),

\displaystyle[\mathcal{Q}_{-2}T^{\leq 2},\mathbf{P}_{\ell\geq 4}]\widehat{\psi}_{-2}

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{Q}_{-2},\mathbf{P}_{\ell\geq 4}]T^{\leq 2}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]

\displaystyle=[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}+4ia\cos\theta T,\mathbf{P}_{\ell\geq 4}]T^{\leq 2}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]

\displaystyle=[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta,\mathbf{P}_{\ell\geq 4}]T^{2}T^{\leq 2}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]+4ia[\cos\theta,\mathbf{P}_{\ell\geq 4}]TT^{\leq 2}\mathrm{Err}[\widehat{\psi}_{-2}]

which is bounded by
|u|^{-8-\delta/2}
in
L^{2}(S(u,+\infty))
by Propositions
4.11
and
4.21
. By (
5.110
), to conclude the proof of Theorem
3.7
, it is only left to prove the following bound, in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
:

|(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}(r,u,\theta,\phi_{-})-(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}(r_{-},u,\theta,\phi_{-})|\lesssim r-r_{-}.

To this end, we use Propositions
4.11
and
4.21
again, from which we deduce in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}

\displaystyle|\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{Q}_{-2}T^{\leq 2}\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}T^{\leq 2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+|u|^{-8-\delta/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{r_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\leq 1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}T^{\leq 2}\widehat{\psi}_{-2}\|_{L^{2}(S(u,{\underline{u}}))}+|u|^{-8-\delta/2}\lesssim 1.

Integrating the bound above in
\mathbf{II}\cup\mathbf{III}
from
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
, and using (
5.110
) on
\mathcal{CH_{+}}
, we obtain the bound

|(\widehat{\psi}_{-2})_{\ell\geq 4}(r,u,\theta,\phi_{-})|\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2}+\int_{r_{-}}^{r}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\lesssim|u|^{-8-\delta/2}+r-r_{-},

which concludes the proof of Theorem
3.7
.

Appendix A
Computations for the energy method

A.1
Integration by parts on the spheres

Lemma A.1
.

Equation (
4.10
) can be rewritten as follows,

\displaystyle\partial_{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\right)+\partial_{u}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\right)+\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu=\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mu\mathfrak{R}\left(\overline{X(\widehat{\psi})}O({\underline{u}}^{-\beta})\right)\mathrm{d}\nu,

where

\mathbf{F}_{\underline{u}}[\widehat{\psi}]:=2(r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\mu g(r)(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})+a\sin\theta\mu\mathfrak{R}\left(\overline{X(\widehat{\psi})}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\right),

\mathbf{F}_{u}[\widehat{\psi}]:=2(r^{2}+a^{2})g(r)|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\mu f(r)(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})-a\sin\theta\mu\mathfrak{R}\left(\overline{X(\widehat{\psi})}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\right),

and the bulk term is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle=2(r\mu f(r)-\partial_{\underline{u}}((r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)))|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+2(r\mu g(r)-\partial_{u}((r^{2}+a^{2})g(r)))|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{u}(\mu f(r))+\partial_{\underline{u}}(\mu g(r)))(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+\mu g(r)\frac{s\mu ra^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})

\displaystyle\quad+4\mu g(r)as\cos\theta\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})-4sr\mu g(r)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})+4g(r)cs(r-M)|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2g(r)ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\Phi\widehat{\psi})+g(r)\left(\mu s-s^{2}\mu\frac{a^{4}\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\mu V\right)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{\psi})

\displaystyle\quad-2sg(r)\mu\frac{a^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})+2r\mu g(r)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi})

\displaystyle\quad+\mu f(r)\frac{s\mu ra^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})-2sf(r)\mu\frac{a^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})

\displaystyle\quad+4\mu f(r)as\cos\theta\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})-4sr\mu f(r)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})+f(r)(2r\mu+4cs(r-M))\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2f(r)ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\Phi\widehat{\psi})+f(r)\left(\mu s-s^{2}\mu\frac{a^{4}\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\mu V\right)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{\psi}).

Proof.

We compute

\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}\left(f(r)\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mu\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(c,V)}\widehat{\psi}\right)+\mathfrak{R}\left(g(r)\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mu\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(c,V)}\widehat{\psi}\right)\mathrm{d}\nu

by integration by parts on
S(u,{\underline{u}})
. First, using (
4.9
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}\Big(f

\displaystyle(r)\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mu\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(c,V)}\widehat{\psi}\Big)\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4(r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu+\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle+\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}\left(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi})\right)\mathrm{d}\nu+4\mu f(r)as\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\cos\theta\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle+f(r)(2r\mu+4s(r-M))\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu+2f(r)r\mu\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle-4sr\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu-\frac{2f(r)ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\Phi\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle+f(r)\left(\mu s-s^{2}\mu\frac{a^{4}\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle-2sf(r)\mu\frac{a^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu.

We have

\displaystyle 4(r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)

\displaystyle\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu=2(r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)\partial_{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{\underline{u}}\left(2(r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)-2\partial_{\underline{u}}((r^{2}+a^{2})f(r))\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu.

Next, using Lemma
2.9
, as well as

[\widehat{e_{3}},\mathcal{U}]=\frac{1}{2}\mu\partial_{r}\left(\frac{\Sigma}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)=\frac{is\mu ra^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}},

we get, in view of
T=\partial_{\underline{u}}-\partial_{u}
,

\displaystyle\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}

\displaystyle(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=T\left(\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}a\sin\theta\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)-\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\mathcal{U}\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=(\partial_{\underline{u}}-\partial_{u})\left(\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}a\sin\theta\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{u}\left(\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{u}(\mu f(r))\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu+\mu f(r)\frac{s\mu ra^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu,

where we used
\partial_{u}=\widehat{e_{3}}+a/(r^{2}+a^{2})\Phi
. We also have, using
[\widehat{e_{3}},\partial_{\theta}]=0
,

\displaystyle\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}\Bigg(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}

\displaystyle(\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi})\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=-\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}\left(\partial_{\theta}\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}\right)\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\widehat{e_{3}}(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mu f(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\right)+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{e_{3}}(\mu f(r))\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu,

and we put the remaining terms in the bulk term
\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]
. Now using the expression (
4.8
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(g(

\displaystyle r)\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mu\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(c,V)}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4(r^{2}+a^{2})g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{e_{3}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu+\mu g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle+\mu g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}\left(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi})\right)\mathrm{d}\nu+4\mu g(r)as\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\cos\theta\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle+g(r)(2r\mu+4s(r-M))\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu+2g(r)r\mu\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle-4sr\mu g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu+\frac{2g(r)ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\Phi\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle+g(r)\left(\mu s-s^{2}\mu\frac{a^{4}\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle-2sg(r)\mu\frac{a^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu.

As before, we have:

\displaystyle 4(r^{2}+a^{2})g(r)

\displaystyle\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{e_{3}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu=2(r^{2}+a^{2})g(r)\partial_{u}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\left(2(r^{2}+a^{2})g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)-2\partial_{u}((r^{2}+a^{2})g(r))\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu,

\displaystyle\mu g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{R}\Bigg(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\frac{1}{\sin\theta}

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}(\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi})\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=\partial_{\underline{u}}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mu g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\right)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\underline{u}}(\mu g(r))\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu,

\displaystyle\mu g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=(\partial_{\underline{u}}-\partial_{u})\left(\mu g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}a\sin\theta\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\underline{u}}\left(\mu g(r)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\underline{u}}(\mu g(r))\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\>\mathrm{d}\nu+\mu g(r)\frac{s\mu ra^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\>\mathrm{d}\nu.

The remaining terms are put in the bulk term, which concludes the proof of Lemma
A.1
.
∎

A.2
Coercivity of the bulk term

Lemma A.2
.

For
\alpha>1
,
s=-2
,
c>0
,
V\in\mathbb{R}
, for
p(a,M)>0
large enough, and
r_{\mathfrak{b}}(p,a,M)
close enough to
r_{-}
, we have in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu\gtrsim(-\mu)\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha+1}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu.

(A.1)

Proof.

As in
[
24
]
, we compute

\displaystyle r\mu g(r)-\partial_{u}((r^{2}+a^{2})g(r))

\displaystyle=-\mu pr\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\right)^{p},

\displaystyle r\mu f(r)-\partial_{\underline{u}}((r^{2}+a^{2})f(r))

\displaystyle=-\alpha(r-M-r\mu)|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha-1},

\displaystyle\widehat{e_{3}}(\mu f(r))+\partial_{\underline{u}}(\mu g(r))

\displaystyle=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r-M-r\mu)\mu(1+\alpha|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-1})|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha}

\displaystyle\quad+\mu(r-M+(p-1)r\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p-1}.

Thus, taking
r_{\mathfrak{b}}
sufficiently close to
r_{-}
such that

M-r+r\mu\gtrsim 1,\quad r\in[r_{-},r_{\mathfrak{b}}],

we then have

\displaystyle r\mu f(r)-\partial_{\underline{u}}((r^{2}+a^{2})f(r))

\displaystyle\gtrsim|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha-1},

\displaystyle\widehat{e_{3}}(\mu f(r))+\partial_{\underline{u}}(\mu g(r))

\displaystyle\gtrsim-\mu|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha}-\mu(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}.

We now define the principal bulk term as

\displaystyle{\mathbf{B}}_{pr}[\widehat{\psi}]:=2(r\mu g(

\displaystyle r)-\partial_{u}((r^{2}+a^{2})g(r)))|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+2(r\mu f(r)-\partial_{\underline{u}}((r^{2}+a^{2})f(r)))|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{1}{2}(\partial_{u}(\mu f(r))+\partial_{\underline{u}}(\mu g(r)))(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}).

We have shown that in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{B}}_{pr}[\widehat{\psi}]\gtrsim(-\mu)p

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha-1}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(-\mu)(|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha}+(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p})(|\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}).

(A.2)

The only thing left to prove is that we can take
p
large enough and
r_{\mathfrak{b}}
sufficiently close to
r_{-}
such that
{\mathbf{B}}[\widehat{\psi}]-{\mathbf{B}}_{pr}[\widehat{\psi}]
can be absorbed in
{\mathbf{B}}_{pr}[\widehat{\psi}]
after integrating on
{S(u,{\underline{u}})}
. This is due to the following mix between weighted Cauchy-Schwarz of the type
|ab|\leq({\varepsilon a^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}b^{2}})/{2}
and the Poincaré inequality (
2.20
). We first deal with the terms with a factor
g(r)
. We have the bounds:

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mu g(r)\frac{sra^{2}\mu\cos\theta\sin\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(-\mu)^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu+\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\hskip-8.5359pt|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim(-\mu)^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}4\mu g(r)as\cos\theta\mathfrak{I}

\displaystyle(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})-4srg(r)\mu\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg|\lesssim

\displaystyle(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu+(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\varepsilon\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu,

where we used (
2.16
) which yields
T=O(\mu)e_{3}+O(1)\partial_{\underline{u}}+O(1)\mathcal{U}+O(1).
We continue with

|2r\mu g(r)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi})|\lesssim(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2},

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\dfrac{2g(r)ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\Phi\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg|\lesssim

\displaystyle(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu+(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\varepsilon\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu,

where we used (
2.17
) which yields
\Phi=O(\mu)e_{3}+O(1)\partial_{\underline{u}}+O(1)\mathcal{U}+O(1)
. Next, we bound

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}g(r)\Bigg(\mu s-s^{2}\mu

\displaystyle\frac{a^{4}\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\mu V\Bigg)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg|\lesssim

\displaystyle(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu+(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\varepsilon\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu,

\displaystyle\Bigg|2sg(r)\mu\dfrac{a^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\Bigg|\lesssim(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\varepsilon^{-1}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+(-\mu)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}\varepsilon|\mathcal{U}\Psi|^{2}.

The only term with a factor
g(r)
in the bulk term that remains to be controlled is

4g(r)cs(r-M)|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}.

Note that with the choice of negative spin
s=-2
, for
c>0
and
r_{\mathfrak{b}}\in(r_{-},M)
this term is non-negative in
\{r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}\}
, and even satisfies

\displaystyle 4g(r)cs(r-M)|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\gtrsim(r^{2}+a^{2})^{p}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}.

(A.3)

Next, we control the terms of the bulk term with a factor
f(r)
. We have

\Bigg|\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mu f(r)\frac{sra^{2}\mu\cos\theta\sin\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg|\lesssim(-\mu)^{2}|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu,

|2r\mu f(r)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi})|\lesssim(-\mu)^{2}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}.

As before, using (
2.16
) and (
2.17
) gives

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}4\mu f(r)as\cos\theta\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})-4srf(r)

\displaystyle\mu\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}T\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(-\mu)^{2}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu+|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}-\dfrac{2f(r)ar\mu}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\Phi\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(-\mu)^{2}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu+|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}f(r)\Bigg(\mu s-s^{2}\mu\dfrac{a^{4}\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\mu V\Bigg)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\widehat{\psi})\mathrm{d}\nu\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(-\mu)^{2}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}\mathbf{e}_{deg}[\widehat{\psi}]\mathrm{d}\nu+|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\nu,

\displaystyle\Bigg|2sf(r)\mu\dfrac{a^{2}\sin\theta\cos\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\mathfrak{I}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi})\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(-\mu)^{2}|\mathcal{U}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2},

\displaystyle|4cs(r-M)f(r)\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi})|

\displaystyle\lesssim|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}\varepsilon^{-1}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\varepsilon|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}.

(A.4)

All these bounds show that we can first chose
p=p(a,M)
large enough, and
\varepsilon>0
small enough at each step, such that all the terms with a factor
g(r)
in
\mathbf{B}[\widehat{\psi}]-\mathbf{B}_{pr}[\widehat{\psi}]
can be absorbed in the RHS of (
A.2
). Then,
p
is fixed and the remaining bounds on the
f(r)
terms, except (
A.4
), can be absorbed in the RHS of (
A.2
), chosing
r_{\mathfrak{b}}
sufficiently close to
r_{-}
, where we use

\displaystyle 0<-\mu\ll 1,\quad 0<-\mu\ll|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha-1},\quad|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}\ll|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha-1},

for
r_{-}\leq r\leq r_{\mathfrak{b}}
with
r_{\mathfrak{b}}
close to
r_{-}
and
\alpha>1
.
The last remaining term that is not directly absorbed in (
A.2
) using the inequalities above is (
A.4
). To treat this term, we use the positive term (
A.3
). More precisely, now that
p
if fixed, choosing
\varepsilon>0
small enough in (
A.4
), we can absorb the term
\varepsilon|\partial_{\underline{u}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}
in (
A.3
), and finally using
|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}\ll|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-\alpha-1}
we can absorb the term

|{\log(-\mu)}|^{-2\alpha}\varepsilon^{-1}|\widehat{e_{3}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}

in (
A.2
). This implies the lower bound (
A.1
), and hence concludes the proof of Lemma
A.2
.∎

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
5.14

We search for a solution
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)
of (
5.83
) as a polynomial of the type

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)=(r-r_{-})^{\ell-2}+\sum_{k=0}^{\ell-3}a_{k}(r-r_{-})^{k}.

Note that for such a choice of
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)
, for any family
(a_{k})
, the coefficient in front of
(r-r_{-})^{\ell-2}
in the LHS of (
5.83
) is

(\ell-2)(\ell-3)+6(\ell-2)+\lambda_{\ell}=(\ell-2)(\ell+3)+\lambda_{\ell}=0.

Then
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}
satisfies (
5.83
) if and only if the coefficients in front
(r-r_{-})^{k}
,
0\leq k\leq\ell-3
, in the LHS of (
5.83
) all vanish. Namely, if and only if the following identities are satisfied:

\displaystyle a_{\ell-3}=\dfrac{(\ell-2)(\ell(r_{+}-r_{-})-2iam)}{(\ell-3)(\ell+2)-(\ell-2)(\ell+3)},

\displaystyle a_{k}=\dfrac{(k+1)((k+3)(r_{+}-r_{-})-2iam)}{k(k+5)-(\ell-2)(\ell+3)}a_{k+1},\quad 0\leq k\leq\ell-4.

Solving the system above gives (
5.84
), and the estimate
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\neq 0
near
r=r_{-}
, as

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})=\prod_{j=0}^{\ell-3}\frac{(j+1)((j+3)(r_{+}-r_{-})-2iam)}{j(j+5)-(\ell-2)(\ell+3)}\neq 0.

Next, we prove
3
. Let
r_{\ell}\in(r_{-},r_{+})
be sufficiently close to
r_{-}
such that
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\neq 0
on
[r_{\ell},r_{-}]
. We define, on
[r_{\ell},r_{-})
,

h_{m,\ell}(r):={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\int_{r_{\ell}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

Note that
h_{m,\ell}
solves (
5.83
), and that
h_{m,\ell}
is linearly independent from
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}
on
[r_{\ell},r_{-})
, so that
(h_{m,\ell},{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1})
is a basis of solutions of (
5.83
) on
[r_{\ell},r_{-})
. Moreover,

\displaystyle h_{m,\ell}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})}\int_{r_{\ell}}^{r}{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+O_{(m,\ell)}((r_{+}-r)^{-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{-\Delta^{-2}(r)e^{-2imr_{mod}}}{(4(r_{-}-M)+2iam){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})}+O_{(m,\ell)}((r-r_{-})^{-1})

(B.1)

by Lemma
5.9
. Now, we define

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}(r):={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(2M-r),

(B.2)

and using that
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}
satisfies (
5.83
), we deduce that
\widetilde{v}
satisfies the equation

\Delta\widetilde{v}^{\prime\prime}(r)+(6(r-M)-2iam)\widetilde{v}^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\ell}\widetilde{v}(r)=0,

namely (
5.83
) with
m
replaced by
-m
, so that there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}
such that

\widetilde{v}(r)=c_{1}{}^{(-m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)+c_{2}h_{-m,\ell}(r).

Using (
B.1
) and the boundedness of
\widetilde{v}(r)
at
r=r_{-}
by (
B.2
) and (
5.84
), we get
c_{2}=0
. Moreover,
{}^{(-m,\ell)}\!v_{1}=\overline{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}}
, so we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}(r)=c_{1}\overline{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)}.

(B.3)

Using the fact that
\widetilde{v}(r)={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(2M-r)
and
\overline{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}}
are polynomials of degree
\ell-2
, and matching coefficients in front of
r^{\ell-2}
in (
B.3
), we obtain
c_{1}=(-1)^{\ell-2}
, and thus

{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(2M-r)=(-1)^{\ell-2}\overline{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)}.

In particular, this implies
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{+})=(-1)^{\ell-2}\overline{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})}\neq 0,
and thus
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\neq 0
near
r=r_{+}
. We have proven for now
1
,
2
, and
3
. We now prove
4
. Multiplying by
\overline{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)}
the ODE (
5.83
) satisfied by
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}
and taking the real part, we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{R}\left(\left(\Delta{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)+(2iam+6(r-M)){}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\ell}{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\right)\overline{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\right)+6(r-M)\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}\right|^{2}-\frac{(\ell-2)(\ell+3)}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\Delta\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\right)+5(r-M)\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}\right|^{2}-\frac{(\ell-2)(\ell+3)}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}\right|^{2}\right),

where we used
\Delta^{\prime}(r)=2(r-M)
. Integrating between
r_{-}
and
r
, we get

\frac{(\ell-2)(\ell+3)}{2}\left(\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})\right|^{2}-\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\right|^{2}\right)=-\frac{\Delta}{2}\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r)\right|^{2}+5\int_{r_{-}}^{r}(M-r^{\prime})\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}^{\prime}(r^{\prime})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}.

Using
\Delta\leq 0
, we deduce
\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})\right|^{2}-\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\right|^{2}\geq 0
for
r\in[r_{-},M]
, and thus

\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{-})\right|\geq\left|{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\right|,\quad r\in[r_{-},M].

By
3
, we get that the bound above holds also for
r\in[M,r_{+}]
, hence
4
. We now define the function
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}
. Let
\varepsilon_{(m,\ell)}>0
such that
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\neq 0
on
[r_{-},r_{-}-2\varepsilon)\cup[r_{+}-2\varepsilon,r_{+}]
. Let
r_{0}\in(r_{-}+\varepsilon,r_{-}+2\varepsilon)
, so that
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r_{0})\neq 0
. The scalar
\Delta
is nowhere-vanishing on
I_{(m,\ell)}:=(r_{-}+\varepsilon,r_{+}-\varepsilon)
, so the ODE (
5.83
) admits a unique solution
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)
defined on
I_{(m,\ell)}
such that

\displaystyle{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r_{0})=0,\quad{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}^{\prime}(r_{0})=\frac{-\Delta^{3}(r_{0})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r_{0})}}{v_{1}(r_{0})}.

Then, the differential equation satisfied by the Wronskian
W_{(m,\ell)}
implies that on
I_{(m,\ell)}
,

\displaystyle W_{(m,\ell)}=-\Delta^{3}e^{-2imr_{mod}}.

(B.4)

We now extend smoothly
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)
to
(r_{-},r_{-}+\varepsilon)
by

\displaystyle{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}(r)={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime},\quad r\in(r_{-},r_{-}+\varepsilon),

(B.5)

where the extension is smooth by
2
and (
B.4
). Finally, let
r_{1}\in[r_{+}-2\varepsilon,r_{+}-\varepsilon)
. We extend
{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}
to
[r_{+}-\varepsilon,r_{+})
by

\displaystyle{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{2}={}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r)\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\frac{\Delta^{-3}(r^{\prime})e^{-2imr_{mod}(r^{\prime})}}{{}^{(m,\ell)}\!v_{1}(r^{\prime})^{2}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+\frac{v_{2}(r_{1})}{v_{1}(r_{1})}v_{1}(r),\quad r\in(r_{+}-\varepsilon,r_{+}),

(B.6)

where the extension is smooth by
2
and (
B.4
). This definition ensures that
1
,
2
,
3
hold. Moreover,
4
holds by (
B.5
) and (
B.6
), similarly as in (
B.1
). This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.14
.

Appendix C
